It is STRUCTURALLY keyed with encounter-ordered entry LISTS, reproducing the old scans' first-match + fallback-to-next-match semantics byte-identically (structurally-equal specifiers in different files legitimately share a key) — do not "optimize" it to identity keys or single entries. `moduleSpecifierCache` stays a lazy memo (its key domain is OPEN — `import("spec")` type positions / dynamic imports) and is worker-local by construction (Checker field, Tier 2); it assumes `fileResults` is frozen after construction.
- **The bare `resolveModuleSpecifier(spec)` only knows FLAT corpus-style file keys — for a path-shaped layout (`/proj/src/f1.ts` importing `./lib`) it is null, so every alias-HOP chain must include `resolveModuleSpecifierRelative(spec, contextFile)` (round 511: its absence in `resolveImportedSymbolGeneral` killed ALL import-mediated typing on real on-disk projects post-INV.3(d) — pre-retire the merge masked the miss; round 512 closed the same hole in `resolveAlias`'s ImportDeclaration AND ImportSpecifier branches via `owningSourceFile(decl)` + the relative leg; round 513 in `namespaceAliasMemberSymbol`'s three arms). The tsc-source profiles CANNOT catch this class (their specifiers are all ESM-`.js` → the separate stripping leg), so compiler/services listAll byte-identity is NOT sufficient verification for module-resolution changes — verify with a path-shaped scratch project via MainKt (cheap, decisive) and keep the direct-construction pins (EnclosingImportIndexTest) green.**
- **`node_modules/*.d.ts` files ARE parsed + bound + in `binderResults`, and their diagnostics DO reach the output** (despite a driver code comment saying node_modules files are "third-party and never reported on" — that comment is about EMIT, not diagnostics; `parsedSourceFiles[fileName]` is set BEFORE the emit-skipping `continue`). Confirmed by the passing `augmentExportEquals7` (reports a node_modules TS2649) and used by B443's `checkGlobalNamespaceMemberConflicts`. So a cross-file checker walk CAN emit diagnostics at node_modules positions — don't assume they're filtered.
- **A cross-file `declare global { namespace NS { … } }` merge is NOT modeled by the binder's symbol tables** — detecting a member-name CONFLICT across files in such a namespace (e.g. `type VNode` in one file vs `export import VNode` in another → TS2300) needs an AST-based dedicated cross-file walker (`checkGlobalNamespaceMemberConflicts`, B443), like the other cross-file conflict walkers. FP firewall: ≥2 DISTINCT files AND a type-alias in the mix (type-aliases are the only kind that can never merge).
- **Function-call arity (TS2554) is cross-file via an OVERLAY, not per-file (B434, `checkArgumentCounts`)**: a per-file `funcParams` map only sees the current file's functions, so a SCRIPT-mode cross-file call (`f()` in B where `function f` is in A) was never arity-checked. A program-wide `crossFileFuncs` overlay (top-level FunctionDeclarations from all NON-module non-.d.ts files) is merged as a FALLBACK for NON-module files. Load-bearing FP firewall: merge ONLY for callees absent from BOTH the file's own funcParams AND its binder `locals` (a local binding of any kind shadows a cross-file function — without this guard a local non-function `f` + `f(1)` FPs); cross-file DUP names (same name declared in ≥2 files) are dropped (conservative). TS6210/TS6211 related-info must resolve against the DECLARING file (`FuncParamInfo.declFileName/declSource` → `emitTS2554TooFew(relSource, relFileName)`), else the param position is computed against the CALLER's source (wrong file+offset). Companion: JS-param optionality is JSDoc-aware — `paramInfo(params, isJsFile, jsDocRequiredParamNames(comments))`; a JS param is optional UNLESS a non-bracket `@param name` tag documents it (`@param [name]` stays optional).
- **Call resolution respects lexical SHADOWING (M1.11) — a NEW fn-body descent site in the arity walker (`checkArgCountIn*`) or the call-types walker must re-apply it, or wrong-signature TS2554/TS2345 FPs return**: arity — `minusParamShadowedNames` at every fn-body descent + the `argCountFnDepth`-gated body-local var removal (the depth gate keeps top-level B64.2 var-arrow checks alive); `collectFuncDecls` deliberately does NOT flatten ModuleDeclaration bodies (namespace fns are a body-scoped overlay collected at the walker's ModuleDeclaration branch); ctor OVERLOADS record an isOverloaded arity RANGE (never just the first sig); a SPREAD argument suppresses too-FEW only (expansion ≥0 → too-many stands). Type path — `populateParameterLocalTypes` infers un-annotated fn-valued-DEFAULT params from the initializer; `shadowNestedFunctionNames` (after the fn-body `currentLocalTypes` copy, call-types walker) anyType-bails body-nested fns colliding with an outer binding (B83.5 leaves them unbound → the globals fallback finds the wrong decl). **Round 429 (all anyType/suppression-only): `applyCallTypesBodyLocalShadowing` at every call-types fn-entry overrides an INHERITED `currentLocalTypes` entry colliding with a body-local decl (a nested fn's `let host = …` vs the enclosing `createTypeChecker(host: TypeCheckerHost)` param — the round-428d branch is gated entry==null so it can't override; a same-fn param redeclaration is excluded, param wins); the ArrowFunction/FunctionExpression branches scope `currentLocalTypes`+`currentParamBindingNames` and register anyType for own param names (the walker deliberately doesn't TYPE arrow params, so a colliding name previously leaked the enclosing binding's type into arg positions).**
- **A lib+MODULE-interface merged symbol's LIB-only members are phantom (round 471, `isLibPhantomMemberOfModuleInterface`)**: a module file's own top-level `interface Symbol` (tsc compiler/types.ts) merges with the same-named LIB global, so the merged interface demands lib members (`description`) tsc never requires. The helper (gated on `moduleInterfaceNames` + lib decls on the symbol + lib-only prop declarations) is wired into propertiesRelatedTo / collectMissingProperties / getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol / the TS2420 implements loop — EXTEND it at a new missing-member verdict site, don't re-derive; script-file lib augmentations don't trip the gate.
- **`mergeSymbolTable` mutates the existing target symbol's `declarations` list**: When `target[name]` already exists, the helper does `existing.flags |= symbol.flags; existing.declarations.addAll(symbol.declarations)` — so calling it twice across the same `result.locals` corpus duplicates declarations on the SHARED symbol object. The init-block merge into `globals` already does this once for every cross-file same-name pair; any additional pass building per-file scope tables MUST NOT re-merge across binderResults via this helper. Use direct `map[name] = sym` assignment with first-write-wins for shared pools and last-write-wins for own-file overrides — no `addAll`, no symbol mutation. Failure mode is silent until a downstream check (e.g. TS6133 unused-T-param, declaration count) sees the duplicated declaration list and behaves incorrectly.
- **`isValueExport`/`isTypeOnlyExportName` cross-file lookups must NOT rely on symbol flags**: `mergeSymbolTable` pollutes the target file's `locals["X"]` symbol with flags merged in from importing files (e.g., `import { T } from "./type"; const T = 0` in file A causes /type.ts's T symbol to acquire BlockScopedVariable|Alias|ExportValue flags). Code that asks "is X exported from file Y as a value or type?" MUST scan `targetResult.sourceFile.statements` directly for declarations of X, not consult `targetResult.locals[X].flags`. Mirror pattern in `isValueExport` (Checker.kt:955) walks the AST and classifies each declaration's kind. Fall back to flag-based logic only when no direct declaration is found (ambient/re-export cases).

### Binder gotchas

- **A DOTTED `namespace X.Y.Z` records its INNERMOST segment symbol (`Z`) for the decl node** — `recordNodeSymbol` overwrites once per segment in `bindModuleDeclaration`'s loop, so `nodeToSymbol[decl]` is the LAST (innermost) segment, and same-named segments MERGE across declaration blocks. So `buildNamespaceScope`'s dotted branch (B512, Checker.kt) MUST resolve via `nodeToSymbol[stmt]` + walk `symbol.parent` `segCount` steps to add each segment's merged exports — NOT root the walk at `result.locals[firstSegment]` (the old code), which finds NOTHING when the dotted namespace is NESTED in another namespace (the first segment lives in the parent's exports, not file locals) → a merged sibling-block member is invisible → spurious TS2304 (`dottedModuleName`: `v` declared in a sibling `M.N.X.Y.Z` block). Companion: `findDeclarationRelatedInfo`'s TS2728 is OMITTED for a TS2552 suggestion that is an INTERMEDIATE (non-last) segment of a dotted namespace name (tsc gives only the innermost segment a `valueDeclaration`) — the `ModuleDeclaration` branch returns null when `decl.name` is a `PropertyAccessExpression` whose last segment ≠ the suggested name.
- **`declareSymbol` is FIRST-WINS for a Class+Class duplicate (B505), last-wins for every OTHER non-mergeable combo.** tsc keeps the FIRST class as the canonical `scope[name]` binding, so name/member resolution uses the first declaration's members (a later same-named class's members are NOT contributed — `class C{p} … class C{pe}` → `C` resolves to `{p}`, so `c.pe` is TS2339, not `c.p`). The later class still gets its own symbol (returned + node-recorded) for its own diagnostics. **TS2300 duplicate detection is AST-based (`checkDuplicateDeclarations` scans statements), so canonical-symbol choice does NOT affect duplicate detection**, and symbol-id allocation is unchanged (the 2nd symbol is still created) → no boundary id-drift. Gated to Class+Class ONLY: class+interface should MERGE (not first-wins) and var+class/fn+class keep last-wins (other tests depend on it).
- **`canMerge` must include Variable+Module**: `declare const b: T; declare namespace b {}` is valid TypeScript. The binder's `canMerge` must allow `Variable + Module` and `Module + Variable` merging, otherwise the second declaration silently overwrites the first symbol. This matters for `isTypeOnlyImportRequire` which then sees only the namespace and incorrectly elides the `require()`. **BUT (B98.r46): var+namespace merge is allowed ONLY when the namespace is VALUE-FREE (uninstantiated — only types/interfaces).** An INSTANTIATED namespace (one with value exports, e.g. `export var x`) is a SECOND VALUE binding → TS2300 even for `declare var` (`declare var console; namespace console { export var x }` conflicts). The `namespaceVarAllowed` gate (Checker.kt:~19710, duplicate-declaration TS2300/TS2451 path) is `group.filter{namespace}.all{ !isNamespaceInstantiated(it) }` — do NOT re-add a `|| allVarsDeclare` blanket allowance (the old comment "a declare var can merge with any namespace" was wrong; the empty-namespace `b{}` case survives because it's not instantiated).
- **`canMerge` includes Class+Interface (round 332)**: `interface Foo {...}` + `class Foo {...}` — the interface members merge into the class instance type (TS declaration merging). Without it the second decl silently overwrote the first symbol → FP TS2339 on an interface-declared member accessed via `this`/the instance. Full-suite-verified net-zero on its own (the ~20 sibling class+interface-merge tests stay passing).
- **`canMerge` includes Variable+TypeAlias (round 473)**: `type ActionSet = "action::set"` + `const ActionSet: ActionSet` (tsc jsTyping/shared.ts) — without the merge the const OVERWRITES the alias symbol, every `kind: ActionSet` member annotation resolves errorType, and discriminated-union narrowing over such members silently dies.
- **`canMerge` must include Variable+Interface**: `interface Symbol {}` + `declare var Symbol: SymbolConstructor;` is the standard wrapper-type pattern in lib.d.ts. Without this merge, the var silently overwrites the interface — `Symbol` resolves to anyType in type position and `sym = Sym` skips type checking. Same gap blocks any user `class B extends A` where A is `interface A {}` + `declare var A: { new(): A }` (e.g. `staticMismatchBecauseOfPrototype`). Note: enabling this merge also makes lib types like `Object`/`Promise` resolve to real Type.Interface (was anyType), exposing two latent gaps in the relation engine — see `propertiesRelatedTo` OBJECT_PROTOTYPE_PROPERTIES filter and async return-type Promise unwrapping below.
- **Ambient module symbol overwritten by import alias**: `import x = require("X")` in the same file as `declare module "X"` creates an Alias symbol that overwrites the NamespaceModule in `locals["X"]` (Alias doesn't canMerge with Module). `isAmbientModuleTypeOnly` must analyze the ModuleBlock AST directly instead of looking up `locals[name]`.
- **Module augmentation vs definition**: `declare module "X"` in a module file (has imports/exports) is an augmentation. In a script file, it's a definition. `isAmbientModuleTypeOnly` must skip module files to avoid treating augmentations (which only add types) as the full module definition.
- **A `namespace`/`module` symbol does NOT carry bare `SymbolFlags.Module` — it carries `NamespaceModule` (uninstantiated) or `ValueModule` (has value members)**: a check that gates "is this a namespace?" on `flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Module)` SILENTLY returns false for most real namespaces (e.g. one with a `class`/`var` member is `ValueModule`). Use `hasAny(SymbolFlags.Module or SymbolFlags.NamespaceModule or SymbolFlags.ValueModule)` (cf. Checker.kt:71904/77421/4755). Symptom: a new namespace-detection walker fires for NOTHING (B393's first run was net-zero because of exactly this).
- **`SymbolFlags.ConstEnum` is set on namespaces holding only const enums**: `bindModuleDeclaration` sets `SymbolFlags.ConstEnum or NamespaceModule` when `ModuleInstanceState.ConstEnumOnly`. Cascades up: `namespace A { namespace B { namespace C { const enum E {...} } } }` makes A, B, C all carry `SymbolFlags.ConstEnum`. So `flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Enum)` (= ConstEnum or RegularEnum) returns TRUE for these namespaces. Any code that distinguishes "real enum" from "namespace" must check `declarations.any { it is EnumDeclaration }` rather than relying on the flag alone.
- **Block-scoped declarations are NOT bound** (B83.5, 2026-05-27): `Binder.kt:bindStatement` only dispatches symbol-declaration cases via explicit `when` arms (Variable/Function/Class/Interface/TypeAlias/Enum/Module/Import/Export). The catch-all `else` branch silently ignores `WhileStatement`/`Block`/`IfStatement`/`ForStatement`/`TryStatement`/`SwitchStatement` and every other non-declaration statement, so any `class X` / `interface I` / `type T` / `enum E` / `function F` declared INSIDE their bodies is NEVER bound to file `locals` or `globals`. Consequence: any checker walker that looks up such a declaration by name via `globals[name]` / `result.locals[name]` / `perFileScope[file]?.get(name)` returns null. Symptom: TS2339/TS2551/TS2693/TS2304 silently never fires inside block-scoped class bodies because `classType = null`. Surgical fix pattern when the AST is in-hand: synthesize a transient `Symbol(SymbolFlags.Class, stmt.name.text)` with `declarations = [stmt]` and pass to `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol)`. The synthetic symbol must NOT be published to binder tables (`globals.put` / `result.locals.put`) — it's a checker-pass-local temporary; cross-decl merging or multi-pass identity is the binder's job. Used in `checkPropertyAccessInStatement`'s ClassDeclaration branch (Checker.kt:~61539); extend to similar `globals[stmt.name.text]`-based lookups if more shapes surface (other checker walkers that recurse into block bodies but resolve nested decls by name).
- **TS2451-vs-TS2300 for a let/const + var collision is decided by the FIRST declaration's kind** (duplicate pipeline `useTs2451`, Checker.kt ~28605): a block-scoped (let/const) declared FIRST → TS2451; a `var` declared first → TS2300. A `function` declaration anywhere in the SAME scope as the let also forces TS2300 (so let+var → TS2451 but let+var+function → TS2300).
### Type system gotchas

- **`checkReturnAssignability`'s engine block does NOT early-return when the relation PASSES for a non-nullish source — control falls to the STRING fallback, which re-widens literals and can FP (round 436c)**: engine-verified early returns are gated to PRECISE verdicts only (nullish M1.9, syntactic-literal-union-member `returnUnionSyntacticallyContainsLiteral`, all-leaves-verified ternary via `checkConditionalReturnBranches`'s tri-state, and round 438's **empty-object-member** verdict — `targetHasEmptyObjectMember(targetType) && checkTypeRelatedTo(source, target)`: a non-nullish source is always assignable to a target carrying an empty-object `{}` member (`{} | undefined`, tsc's `getOptionValueWithEmptyStrings(...): {} | undefined` returning `""`), and round 430's empty-object rule is sound there, so the engine is trustworthy). A blanket engine-confirmed return would suppress the string path's catches in M3-gap areas — extend the precise-verdict list instead.
- **The union-target decomposition frame is TRANSPARENT on the relation source stack (round 435e)**: `structuredTypeRelatedTo`'s Union-target branch pops its own redundant source-stack entry around the member iteration (re-pushed in finally) so the same-target arg-shortcut's `isReentry` doesn't misread `Ref<A>` vs `Ref<B> | undefined` as a re-entry — keep the pop/re-push pairing, and do NOT "tighten" `isReentry` to require the repeat on BOTH stacks (measured: breaks recursiveTypeComparison — a genuine member-recursion re-entry repeats on the SOURCE side only).
- **Fresh object-literal literal props keep their literal types vs literal-expecting targets (round 435b)**: `propertiesRelatedTo` retries a failing literal-containing target member with the un-widened literal recovered from the member symbol's PropertyAssignment, ONLY within `freshObjLitRange` (set via `withFreshObjLitSource` at the var-decl / assignment / conditional-return consumers). A new fresh-literal relation consumer must wrap its relation call; the freshness gate is LOAD-BEARING — a widened var reference must keep failing (tsc widens non-contextual const literals). **Round 448: `checkReturnAssignability` retries the coarse relation with `withFreshObjLitSource` for an object literal returned against a UNION target — a discriminated-union return (`return { type: "cases" }` vs `... | { type: "cases"; } | ...`) widens the discriminant to its base primitive (`{ type: string }`), matching no member; the retry recovers the un-widened literal per member so it relates. Suppression-only (only when the retry PASSES).**
- **A GENERATOR's `return expr` checks the annotation's TReturn, never the iterator type (round 435)**: `checkReturnAssignability`'s top gate (`inGeneratorFunctionBody`, threaded like `isAsync` through `checkFunctionBody`) unwraps a Generator-family reference's explicit 2nd type arg and SKIPS otherwise (single-arg/alias forms have TReturn = any). A new return-check emitter must respect the flag or the bare-`return;`-in-generator FP returns.
- **An assignment TARGET checks against the DECLARED type, never the narrowed one (M1.9, `narrowedDeclaredTypes`)**: both assignability-walk IfStatement arms overwrite `currentLocalTypes[name]` with the narrowed type for then-branch READS — any new narrowing overwrite of `currentLocalTypes` must also record the pre-narrowing type into `narrowedDeclaredTypes` (save/restore alongside), and any new identifier-WRITE type resolution must consult it first, or `x = undefined` inside `if (x !== undefined)` FPs.
- **Narrowing reference paths carry literal-index element-access segments since round 461 (`decls[0].initializer` → `"decls[0].initializer"`, `getReferencePath`'s ElementAccessExpression arm)** — extract a path's ROOT with `flowPathRoot` (splits on `[` AND `.`), never a bare `substringBefore('.')` (yields `"x[0]"`, silently missing every root-name comparison against binder/localNames sets); segment-boundary prefix tests use `pathPrefixOf` (`.` or `[`). A write `x[0] = …` gates the walk exact-path only (mirroring property-path targets, incl. the same accepted staleness for a nullish exact-path write).
- **Removing an FP can SURFACE a different pre-existing FP at the SAME position (same-position masking)** — M1.9's TS2322 removal at empty `return;` statements exposed 8 masked TS7030s at identical positions. A by-code bench diff showing +N in an unrelated code after an FP fix: check position overlap with the removed family before treating it as a new regression.
- **TS7030 fires ONLY under `noImplicitReturns`; TS2366 only under `strictNullChecks` when undefined is NOT assignable to the RESOLVED return type (M1.8, mirrors tsc `checkAllCodePathsInNonVoidFunctionReturnOrThrow`)**: under strict-only, an undefined-ACCEPTING return draws NOTHING on fall-through, and an empty `return;` routes through return-expression assignability (TS2322), not TS7030 — do not re-add `strictNullChecks` to the TS7030 arms in `checkBodyForImplicitReturn`. `returnAnnotationAcceptsUndefined` = engine relation on a concrete resolution OR `aliasUnionContainsNullishKeyword` (syntactic `| undefined` proof for aliases whose resolution collapses to anyType, e.g. enum-member unions).
- **An OPTIONAL target property `a?: T` elaborates against `T | undefined` ONLY when the SOURCE is NULLISH — a concrete source uses the bare `T` (round 351, `widenOptionalTargetPropType`, gated `strictNullChecks && !exactOptionalPropertyTypes`).** `null ≁ number | undefined` (intersectionsAndOptionalProperties) but `boolean ≁ string` NOT `string | undefined` (intersectionPropertyCheck `x = y` where `y: T & {a:boolean}`, `x: {a?:string}`). The source-nullish gate is LOAD-BEARING: a blanket widening regressed intersectionPropertyCheck (showed `string | undefined` where tsc drills the union to `string`). Also no-ops when the declared type already includes `undefined` (an explicit `a?: string | undefined`). Threaded to FIVE sites (all pass the EFFECTIVE source prop type): `propertiesRelatedTo` (relation result — an `undefined` source then correctly PASSES an optional target), the main elaboration leaf, `getIntersectionPropertyElaborationChain`, `intersectionMergedContradictsTarget`, and (round 440) `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes`' per-property LEAF (a fresh object-literal value `sourceIndex: hasSource ? n : undefined` — `number | undefined` — vs an optional `sourceIndex?: number`, tsc's sourcemap.ts captureMapping; the DISPLAY keeps the bare member type, only the relation widens).
- **`combineBinaryTypes` types `a ?? b` as `NonNullable<a> | b` (round 440) — it STRIPS null/undefined/void from the left operand (pure-nullish left → the right operand only), NOT the naive `a | b`.** `verbosityLevel ?? -1` where `verbosityLevel: number | undefined` is `number`, not `number | undefined` (checker.ts nodeBuilder `maxExpansionDepth`); without the strip the residual `undefined` FP'd against a non-undefined target. `getTypeOfExpression(BinaryExpression)` → `getTypeOfBinaryExpression` (left-spine fold) → `combineBinaryTypes` is the only `??`-result-typing path.
- **`A && B` types as `falsy(A) | B` (round 475)** — the left's [isDefinitelyFalsyMember] members (nullish + `false`/`0`/`""` literals — the EXACT set the `||` branch drops; keep the two symmetric) union into the result (`oldProgram && oldProgram.state` is `State | undefined`), and a string-LITERAL `||` RIGHT operand keeps its literal type when the kept left is all string-literals (`getBaseConfigFileName(…) || "other"`). The string→""/number→0 primitive decomposition is deliberately not modeled.
- **`combineBinaryTypes` narrows the `||`/`&&` RIGHT operand, and the `ConditionalExpression` branch typing narrows the TRUE/FALSE branches, by the governing condition via `narrowOperandByCondition` → `applyConditionNarrowing` (round 458, pure AST — no flow graph).** `A && B` types B under "A is true"; `A || B` (and a ternary FALSE branch) under "A is false"; a ternary TRUE branch under "A is true" — mirroring the binder's FlowCondition on the operand. So `insertComment === undefined || insertComment` types `boolean` (not `boolean | undefined`), and `cond ? ref : …` narrows `ref`. Fires only for a pure Identifier/PropertyAccess operand (`getReferencePath`); FP-safe by construction (the type is returned unchanged when the condition doesn't mention the operand's path). CAVEAT: this is the `getTypeOfExpression` path — RETURN-position ternaries/logicals go through the SEPARATE `checkConditionalReturnBranches` (per-branch), which this does NOT touch. It can EXPOSE a latent whole-program mis-inference (a wrongly-typed reference narrowed "cleaner" then fails a relation the coarse union masked — checker.ts:11321 `name`'s DeclarationName conflation).
- **The intersection-SOURCE relation `source.types.any { relatedTo(it, target) }` is too LENIENT — a constituent can satisfy the target while ANOTHER constituent contributes a PRESENT property whose type contradicts it (round 351, `intersectionMergedContradictsTarget`).** `{a:null} & {b:string}` relates via `{b:string}` (target's `a?` optional, `b` matches), but the merged `a:null ⊄ number | undefined` → TS2322. Bounded to PRESENT props (a MISSING prop stays on the constituent/structural path) and a PLAIN (non-tuple) object target. **Round 465: a member declared by MULTIPLE constituents contradicts only when EVERY declaration fails the (optional-widened) target — tsc gives it the INTERSECTION of the declared types, so if ANY declaration relates, the intersected member (a subtype of each) relates too; a first-decl-wins merge FP'd the tsc cast idiom `node as AssignmentExpression<EqualsToken> & { readonly left: GeneratedIdentifier }` against its own annotation (the interface's WIDE `left` shadowed the TypeLiteral's refinement).** The matching elaboration is the existing `getIntersectionPropertyElaborationChain`.
- **A function-body-local var declaration SHADOWS an outer/file-level binding of the same name, but the assignment-LHS resolution + `checkMemberAccessMissing` resolve the receiver via `globals`/`currentFileLocals` (the OUTER decl) → FP (round 351, `applyBodyLocalShadowing` + `currentShadowedNames`).** `function foo(v){ let x: To; x = v; x.field=…}` where a file-level `declare let x: {…}` exists — `x` inside foo is `To`, not the outer type. `applyBodyLocalShadowing` (called in BOTH the assignability pass `checkFunctionBody` AND the property-access pass FunctionDeclaration branch, AFTER params) records the LOCAL annotation type into `currentLocalTypes` for top-level body var-decls whose name is bound at an outer level (`globals` OR inherited `currentLocalTypes`), and tracks them in `currentShadowedNames`. **LOAD-BEARING: a `var x` redeclaring a same-function PARAM `x` is a REDECLARATION (param/first wins, TS2403), NOT a shadow — exclude param names** (functionArgShadowing: `foo(x:A){ var x:B; x.bar() }` keeps `x: A` → `x.bar()` is TS2339). The assignment-LHS resolution skips `globals` for a shadowed name; `checkMemberAccessMissing` bails (no TS2339) when a shadowed local's type HAS the member. **Round 447: `applyBodyLocalShadowing` is ALSO called in the property-access walker's ArrowFunction AND FunctionExpression branches (a Block body) — a NESTED callback's inner `let/const x: T` must shadow an outer same-named binding for reads inside it (completions.ts's inner `let exportInfo: SymbolExportInfo` vs an outer `const exportInfo: ExportInfoMap` — reads FP'd TS2339 on the outer type). Both branches save/restore `currentShadowedNames` so the inner shadow does not leak outward. Any NEW nested-function-body walk site in the property-access pass must mirror this call, or a shadowing inner local silently resolves to the outer type.** **Round 455: an un-annotated body-local shadowing a GLOBAL (an exported fn merged into globals — core.ts's `clone<T>(object: T): T`/`identity`) now registers `currentLocalTypes[nm] = anyType` (`inGlobals && !inLocal`), was `remove` — a value-position use (`return clone`) otherwise fell through `getTypeOfIdentifier` to the global generic fn → FP `<T>(object: T) => T`; a concrete inferred local still wins (checked first), a file-level VAR shadow (inLocal) keeps the remove-and-re-infer. `applyBodyLocalShadowing` ALSO descends into nested blocks (`applyNestedGlobalShadow`, anyType-only, GLOBAL collisions only — never a concrete annotation type, which would leak the block-local shape function-wide) so `const clone=…;return clone` inside an `if` (expressionToTypeNode.ts:535/checker.ts:15679) resolves correctly.**
- **`getTypeOfFunction`'s param-type loop is SPLIT (round 460): a `this`-param function resolves each symbol's type from its OWN valueDeclaration (the this-dropped paramSymbols shifted every type by one — core.ts multiMapAdd), but a NO-this function KEEPS the positional zip against decl.parameters — a leading BINDING-PATTERN param is also dropped from paramSymbols (round-446 gotcha) and the shift keeps sig.parameters[i] carrying decl.parameters[i]'s type, which call-site arg alignment relies on (an unconditional valueDeclaration-based fix regressed 19 moduleSpecifiers/emitter sites).** The proper fix is placeholder symbols for pattern params (blast radius, queued). `this` params no longer count toward minArgumentCount.
- **A name with ≥2 block-scoped (`let`/`const`) declarations in ONE function body is AMBIGUOUS in the flat first-decl-wins `currentLocalTypes` (round 460, `applyAmbiguousBlockScopedLocals`/`ambiguousBlockLocalNames`)** — it registers anyType at body entry (loop-HEADER decls excluded — per-iteration scoped) and `checkVarDeclAssignability` skips re-recording it in `currentLocalTypes`/`currentLocalDeclTypeNodes`/`varTypes`. Any NEW body-local recording channel must consult the set (program.ts findSourceFileWorker's three `const file` — the final `return file` otherwise read the first if-block's `SourceFile | false | undefined`).
- **`registerNestedGlobalShadowDecls` also registers BINDING-PATTERN element names (round 460)** — a nested `const { start, length } = getSpan(…)` shadows a same-named global (core.ts's `length` fn) under the same anyType-only/global-collision discipline. (The round-442 arithmetic-arm bail in `arithOperandType` is deleted — round 513.)
- **An INTERSECTION-typed receiver's property READ resolves to the intersection of the per-constituent property types (round 352, `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` Intersection branch) — `getPropertyOfType` itself has NO Intersection branch (modifying it is broad), so the branch lives in `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` only.** A property exists if ANY constituent declares it; single contributor → that type, multiple → `getIntersectionType` (so `({a:string}&{a:number}).a` → `string & number` = `never`, matching tsc). So `v.field` where `v: {field:null} & Omit<To,'field'>` resolves to `null` not anyType. The change is BROAD (every intersection-receiver property read was previously anyType) but strictly more correct → ZERO regressions. PAIRED with: `checkPropertyAccessAssignment`'s write-check now widens the optional TARGET prop via `widenOptionalTargetPropType(pt, targetPropSym, valueType)` → `ptForRel` for the relation+display (the null/undefined BAIL keeps the UNWIDENED `pt`, so a `null` source to `field?: number` falls through to the failing relation → `x.field = v.field` fires TS2322 'null' ≁ 'number | undefined').
- **A UNION whose MEMBER is an INTERSECTION (`PropertyAccessExpression | (ElementAccessExpression & Declaration & {…})`, tsc's `BindableStaticAccessExpression`) resolves a property on the intersection arm via `resolveMemberPropertyType` (round 419) — the round-352 Intersection branch above is for a DIRECT intersection receiver ONLY, and `typeHasOwnProperty` bails on a `Type.Intersection` member (`type !is Type.Object`), so a union with an intersection member FP'd TS2339 on a property INHERITED by that arm (`.parent` via `Node`; ~28 binder.ts/utilities.ts sites).** `resolveMemberPropertyType(member, prop)` folds an intersection member's constituents (a property exists on `A & B` iff ANY has it), reducing to plain `getPropertyOfType` for a non-intersection member (minimal blast radius). Wired at TWO reader sites — `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess`'s B83.4e union-member fold (the property TYPE) AND `checkMemberAccessMissing`'s `memberHasIt` (the TS2339 EMISSION); BOTH are needed (the type site alone leaves the emission firing). **COMPANION (LOAD-BEARING): `discriminantPropAnnotation` (switch / `===` discriminant narrowing) ALSO folds intersection constituents — else an intersection union-member with a NON-matching `.kind` SURVIVES a `switch (node.kind) { case … }` (its `.kind` reads as unknown → conservatively kept) and the over-wide narrowed union then FP's TS2339 on the case-body property (utilities.ts `node.moduleSpecifier`, exactly the 3-FP regression the 1st cut introduced). The lesson: the intersection-fold must be applied to EVERY site that reads a member's property (property resolution AND discriminant-narrowing annotation), not just the obvious property-access one.** Do NOT add the fold to `getPropertyOfType` itself — that stays broad-risk per the round-352 rationale. **SIBLING (round 420): the discriminant-narrowing `enumMemberKeysOfTypeNode` must ALSO resolve a bare-Identifier `.kind` annotation that is a TYPE ALIAS to a union of enum members (`ProjectReferenceFile.kind = ProjectReferenceFileKind = FileIncludeKind.Source | FileIncludeKind.Output`) — its TypeReference branch handled only a direct `Enum.Member` (`QualifiedName`), so an alias-kind member's `.kind` read as unknown and it SURVIVED a `switch (x.kind) { case … }` → the over-wide union FP'd TS2339 (watch.ts `reason.typeReference`). It now resolves + recurses the alias body (`currentFileLocals?.get ?: globals` → `TypeAliasDeclaration`, depth-guarded ≤8), mirroring round-415's `enumSwitchKeysFromTypeNode`. Same theme: close the discriminant-reading gap for EVERY discriminant SHAPE (direct enum-member, intersection-member, type-alias-of-enum-members).
- **A type-alias whose body is an intersection of ALL-ANONYMOUS object types (TypeLiterals / materialized `Omit<>`/`Pick<>`, `symbol == null`) IS registered in `aliasDisplayMap` (round 352, `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker` `shouldRegister`) → `From = { field: null } & Omit<To,'field'>` displays as `From`.** A NAMED intersection (`Foo & Bar`, Type.Interface/Reference constituents) stays UNFOLDED per tsc (the load-bearing B50.4 TS2416 case — do NOT register named intersections). The pre-existing Union-constituent branch (`Common & {foo:1}`) is unchanged.
- **Index-signature assignability is asymmetric source-vs-target (B418b)**: a SOURCE index signature does NOT satisfy a TARGET's required NAMED property — `propertiesRelatedTo` must NOT shortcut `sourceProp==null && source.stringIndexInfo!=null` to "present" (`{[k:string]:any}` is NOT assignable to `{one:number}` → TS2741, same as the number-index source). Conversely a PRIMITIVE source vs an ANONYMOUS index-sig-only target relates via the source's APPARENT type: `string`→`{[n:number]:any}` PASSES (String has a numeric index sig) but `string`→`{[s:string]:any}` and `boolean`→`{[n:number]:any}` FAIL — the `structuredTypeRelatedTo` branch must check the apparent type HAS each index sig the target needs (string-index source also covers a numeric-index target). Delegating to `checkTypeRelatedTo(apparent, target)` OVERSHOOTS — `objectTypeRelatedTo`'s index-sig check does NOT enforce source index-sig PRESENCE when the target value type is `any`.
- **`Parameters`/`ReturnType`/`ConstructorParameters` are now MATERIALIZED (B407, `materializeSignatureUtility`, symbol==null dispatch in `getTypeFromTypeReference`)** — `Parameters<F>`/`ConstructorParameters<F>` resolve to a real TUPLE of the sig's param types, `ReturnType<F>` to the return type (not errorType anymore). Additive: returns null (→errorType) for rest/optional params or no matching sig. The TS2344 "does not satisfy `(...args: any) => any`" for `Parameters<typeof Class>` (constructor type, no call sig) is a SEPARATE check (`checkUtilitySignatureConstraint`) gated on the whole ref resolving to errorType.
- **Array-literal→tuple element checking (TS2322) is VAR-DECL-only (`checkArrayLiteralElementsAgainstTuple`, B407)** — the relation engine still SKIPS array→tuple (Checker.kt ~64488 "needs contextual typing, not yet implemented"); the var-decl path now handles the array-LITERAL case element-wise. To cover call-arg/assignment tuple targets, extend that walker (do NOT un-skip the relation engine — array-VARIABLE→tuple is a different, intentionally-conservative path).
- **`getTupleType` COLLAPSES a VARIADIC tuple's rest element to its ARRAY type as a single `tupleElementTypes` entry (B526)**: `[...number[], number]` → `tupleElementTypes = [number[], number]`, so prop "0" is `number[]` (wrong — element access should give `number`). There is NO rest/minLength tracking on the tuple `Type.Object`. So tuple-element semantics (element access past the fixed count, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` `| undefined`) CANNOT be derived from the resolved Type — they need the AST `TupleType` node. `indexedAccessWithVariableElement`'s `const x:number = recv[i]` is handled by a dedicated AST walker (`receiverVariadicTupleNode` + `vtiaCheckVarDecl`, reusing B221's `trduTupleOf`/structure-parse) hooked as a SUPPRESSION guard at the top of `checkVarDeclAssignability` (the collapsed-model standard check FP's on rest-region indices + misses out-of-bounds, so delegating+returning is strictly better). tsc's rule: a literal index `i` gets `| undefined` iff `i >= fixedBefore + fixedAfter` (count of NON-rest elements); the rest's ELEMENT type is the rest `ArrayType`'s `.elementType` (unwrap it — the raw rest node is the array). Any FUTURE variadic-tuple feature (destructuring past min length is the separate B221 `checkTupleRestDestructureUndefinedUse`; call-arg/assignment tuple indexing; tuple length/assignability) must likewise read the AST node, NOT `tupleElementTypes` — or fix the representation to track `fixedBefore`/`fixedAfter`/`restElementType` (broad).
- **NameScope carries type-param CONSTRAINTS (`typeParamConstraintOf`, B406)** — populated at `addTypeParam(name, constraint)` sites; a new `addTypeParam` call should pass `it.constraint` if available. Used to detect e.g. `T extends string[]` for the indexed-access TS2536 check. An invalid string-literal index in an indexed-access TYPE (`string[]["0.0"]`→TS2339, `T["0.0"]`→TS2536) fires ONLY for a numeric-LOOKING-but-non-canonical key (`isNumericLiteralName`: canonical iff `String(Number(s))===s`) on an array-shaped object — never a valid array member/index, so FP-safe.

- **A generic alias substitution SHADOWS its own TP names out of `currentTypeParamScope` for the body resolution (round 472)** — the scope is consulted BEFORE `currentTypeAliasArgs` in `getTypeFromTypeReference`, so a callee TP named like an alias TP (`binarySearchKey<T,U>(…, keyComparer: Comparer<U>)` with `Comparer<T> = (a:T,b:T)=>…`) CAPTURED the alias body's `a: T` → the contextual anchor mapper typed the callback param wrong (FP TS2538, utilities.ts:1750). Do not remove the shadow; a new alias-args-like resolution context must handle same-named scope entries the same way.
- **The negative type-guard branch keeps a reference whose declared `.kind` DOMAIN exceeds the guard target's (`kindDomainProvesNotSubtype`, round 472)** — the relation over-accepts a wide AST-node interface against a brand-kinded token union (`Node <: Modifier` via enum-any), collapsing `!isModifier(node)` to `never`. The node-side domain reader threads type-arg NODES through generic `extends` levels (`ModifierToken<SyntaxKind.X>` → `Token<TKind> { kind: TKind }`); extend it for new token shapes, never bypass.
- **A services FP that fails to reproduce minimally may just need REAL LIBS — put `"lib": ["es2020"]` (the bench tsconfig) in the repro tsconfig before declaring it whole-program-only (round 472)**: `w.items[0]` FP'd TS2339 "Property '0'" under real libs only; a second dissolver is a same-named cross-file top-level fn (add a colliding decl file). For genuinely opaque emissions, a temporary `init {}` probe in the `Diagnostic` data class keyed on (code, start, fileName) printing `Exception().stackTraceToString()` finds the emitter in one run — frame line numbers wrap mod 65536.
- **The EMBEDDED lib is consulted even under `useRealLibs` (`libGlobals = parseBuiltinLib()` feeds perFileScope/property-access paths) — a member absent from the embedded interface FPs TS2339 no matter what the real lib declares (round 472: `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor` was simply missing from the embedded ObjectConstructor).** When a lib member mysteriously misses under real libs while its siblings resolve, grep BUILTIN_LIB_SOURCE for it FIRST; adding it there is the fix (mind the "and N more" count-shift trap and LIB_MIN_TARGET gating for post-es5 members).
Conditional-type `infer` is AST-guided POSITIONAL unification (`tryEvaluateConditionalWithInfer`), not type-level — `getTypeFromTypeNode` yields errorType for infer-bearing extends-types. FP firewall (load-bearing): ONLY the positive-unification path returns non-null; union-checkType / different-target / arity / non-infer mismatches all return null so the caller falls back to the prior anyType behavior. Bindings layer onto `currentTypeAliasArgs` (a non-null map bypasses the nodeTypes cache → fresh resolution). Tuple/array/fn-type infer positions and union distribution are NOT handled.
- **`getTypeFromMappedType`: a UNION constraint with ANY non-string-literal constituent bails the whole mapped type to anyType (round 463)** — `[K in keyof T & Keys | StrictKeys]` with T un-inferred previously enumerated only the literal constituents, and the PARTIAL key domain manufactured an excess-property TS2353 on a valid key (tsc createComputedCompilerOptions). Do not restore partial enumeration.
- **`getTypeFromMappedType` now BINDS the mapped key K to the current key (a `Type.StringLiteral`) via `currentTypeAliasArgs` around the per-key value resolution (round 333) — so mapped-type member VALUES are REAL types, NO LONGER always `anyType`.** `View<T> = { [K in keyof T]: T[K] extends object ? boolean | View<T[K]> : boolean }` resolves `View<TypeA>["bar"]` → `boolean | View<TypeB>` (per-key `T[K]` + conditional eval), where before the value bailed to anyType. Measured EXACTLY net-zero across the suite (making user-mapped-type values real regressed nothing) — but it is a genuine behavior change a future agent must account for (a mapped member type is now meaningful, e.g. for excess-descent/assignability). A HOMOMORPHIC `[K in keyof T]` mapped type ALSO carries each synthesized member's SOURCE property declaration (`homomorphicSourceType` = the resolved `keyof`-operand; member K's `declarations` = T's prop K's decl) so "declared here" TS6500/TS2728 related info points at the original property. Gated `nameType == null` (no `as`-remap). `Pick`/`Omit`/`Partial`/`Required`/`Readonly` are still materialized SEPARATELY (`materializeMemberSetUtility`) — this only affects USER mapped types.
- **Nested object-literal mismatch/excess descent handles INTERSECTION and UNION targets (round 333, excessPropertyChecksWithNestedIntersections)**: `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` accepts an Intersection target (per-prop types via `getTargetPropertyType` which flattens) so a fresh nested objlit vs `B & C` descends to the deepest leaf; `emitPerPropertyMismatchesForObjectLiteral` descends into a fresh nested object-literal property value via `checkNestedObjLitPropTypes` (deepest `number ≁ string` at the inner key) instead of a coarse `getPropertyElaborationChain` at the outer key; `checkExcessProperties`' recursion handles an Intersection nested target (`photo: {id}&{url}` → flatten via `collectTargetPropertyNames`, which now `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` its constituents) AND a Union nested target (`bar: boolean | View<TypeB>` → descend the object constituent via `selectObjectConstituentForObjectLiteral`). The nested-excess TS6500 uses the alias-aware `displayTarget` param (`D`/`MyType`, not expanded). `typeof <value>.<prop>` (a TypeQuery on a VALUE property-chain, e.g. `typeof obj.photo`) resolves via `resolveTypeOfValueEntityName` (`resolveQualifiedName` only reaches namespace/module/class symbols, not a property of a value) and `excessPropDisplayTarget` renders the RESOLVED type for a TypeQuery annotation.
TypeScript UNFOLDS a type-alias that resolves to a PRIMITIVE in error displays (`type N = number; let x: N = "s"` shows 'number', not 'N'); object/interface aliases keep their name. Use `displayTargetAnnotation` — currently wired only at the general var-decl TS2322 site; route any other site through it when it shows an alias name where tsc shows the primitive.
`formatPropertyDisplayName` preserves the SOURCE quote char of a non-identifier string-literal property name (via `nameNode.singleQuote`) — do NOT hardcode one quote style; baselines require both single- and double-quoted renders. Numeric-literal names (`{ 0: 1 }`) are NumericLiteralNode → rendered unquoted.
`elementAccessResultType` distributes over Type.Union receivers ONLY when EVERY member yields a concrete (non-any/error) element type — any anyType member keeps the whole access anyType (load-bearing conservative gate; preserves prior permissive behavior, bounds FP). Companion: TS2769 union-arg chain sub-lines report the NULLISH failing member FIRST (undefined, then null), gated to union-arg-vs-primitive-param (`getUnionMemberFailureSubline`).
`getTypeOfArrayLiteral` dedupes element types STRUCTURALLY (`ts2403Identical`), but `getUnionType` (Checker.kt ~83926) still dedupes by `it.id` only — a union built from structurally-identical-but-distinct Type.Objects keeps duplicates, and a duplicate-member Type.Union receiver SILENTLY bails out of TS2339 in checkMemberAccessMissing. Adding structural dedup to getUnionType is the general fix but it is called everywhere (broad regression risk) — deliberately left scoped to the array-literal path. When a diagnostic mysteriously doesn't fire on a value that 'should' be a single object type, check for a duplicate-member union.
- **Variable initializer inference scope**: `currentLocalTypes` is populated from unannotated variable initializers (widened) during the TS2322 walk. Must skip `null`/`undefined`/`void` initializers to avoid FPs in "declare then assign" patterns. `getTypeFromTypeQuery` (typeof) must NOT use `currentLocalTypes` — function-scoped variables shouldn't be visible in type annotation positions.
- **A synthesized Type.Object whose member SYMBOLS carry side-channel state (`mappedReadonlyMemberIds`, `nonWideningLiteralTypeIds`, etc.) is DESTROYED by `widenType` when it rebuilds member symbols (B435)**: `widenType`'s `Type.Object` branch creates FRESH `Symbol`s for any member whose type widens, with NEW ids absent from those sets — so the side-channel state silently evaporates. An unannotated `const x = <synth-obj-call>()` reaches `widenType` via `getTypeOfSymbol`→`inferTypeFromInitializer`, but the SAME var read via `currentLocalTypes` (set in `checkVarDeclAssignability`'s no-annotation branch) does NOT widen objects (`else -> inferred`) — so a bug here is MASKED on the currentLocalTypes path and only shows on the getTypeOfSymbol path (e.g. a readonly-write TS2540 firing in a read-pass but not in `checkConstAssignmentInExpr`). Fix: register the synthesized type's id in a set (cf. `frozenObjectTypeIds`) and short-circuit `widenType` to return it as-is. `Object.freeze(objLit)` (`tryResolveObjectFreezeCall`) is the reference; tsc never widens `Readonly<T>` anyway. **Round 455: the same rebuild dropped `tupleElementTypes` and widened a tuple's `length` LITERAL (`2 → number`), so a widened tuple `[SF, FR]` became a `{0,1,length:number}` object that failed to relate to a tuple target (`length: number ⊄ 2`, the `map(arr, x => [a, b])` returns-tuple family). widenType now special-cases `tupleElementTypes != null`: widen the ELEMENT types only + rebuild via `buildTupleFromTypes` (length literal + tupleElementTypes + per-element optionality preserved); when no element widens (elements already non-literal) the tuple is returned untouched.**
- **A readonly property WRITE is TS2540 only — never also TS2322 (B435)**: tsc reports the readonly error and types the write reference as `any`, so the assignability check never runs. `checkPropertyAccessAssignment` skips its TS2322 path when the target prop ∈ `mappedReadonlyMemberIds` (the Readonly<T>/freeze synthesized-readonly side-channel — declared `readonly x` MODIFIER members are NOT in that set and keep prior behavior). The separate `checkReadonlyAssignmentTarget` (via `checkConstAssignmentInExpr`) owns the TS2540 emit. The two paths run in DIFFERENT passes with different `currentLocalTypes` state.
- **Interface call/construct signatures are `MethodDeclaration` with special names**: The parser produces `MethodDeclaration(name=Identifier(""))` for call signatures and `MethodDeclaration(name=Identifier("new"))` for construct signatures in interfaces/type literals. `resolveInterfaceMembers` must detect these and add to `callSignatures`/`constructSignatures` lists, NOT as named properties. Otherwise they appear as property "" or "new" and cause FPs in TS2430 and structural comparison.
- **Call/construct signature resolution must push enclosing interface's `typeParameters` into scope**: in `resolveInterfaceMembers`'s call/construct sig branch, set `currentTypeParamScope` to include the interface's TPs (plus any sig-own TPs from `member.typeParameters`) BEFORE resolving return type and parameter type annotations. Pre-resolve param types into `symbolTypes[paramSym.id]` so later `getTypeOfSymbol(paramSym)` returns the in-scope-resolved type after scope restoration. Without this, `T` references in `interface I<T> { (a: T): T; }` resolve through `getTypeFromTypeReference` with null scope → fall to globals → `errorType` (cached in `nodeTypes` because `cacheable = currentTypeParamScope == null`). Downstream `instantiateSignature` calls become no-ops on errorType — silent failure, no diagnostic emitted. Same fix likely needed for any other site that resolves member type annotations from inside an interface/class declaration without pushing the type-param scope.
- **Class construct signatures are static-side only**: `objectTypeRelatedTo` must skip construct signature comparison for class/interface instance types (`isClassInstance` check). Construct signatures on classes belong to the static side (typeof Class), used for `new` expression checking. Comparing construct signatures during assignment causes FPs when source class has no explicit constructor but target does.
- **`getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` bails to `anyType` for a NAMED `Type.Interface` callee with multiple call signatures** — a deliberate, uncommented FP-avoidance gate (Checker.kt, before the overload-selection path). Single-signature and anonymous function types resolve normally; arity-based selection exists via `resolveCallOverload`, but the named-interface bail PRECEDES it.
- **Full Object↔Object comparison is now OPEN (NOT blocked) — the 39+-regression block was resolved by cycle detection (round 348 re-read of `canUseTypeEngine`).** `canUseTypeEngine` returns `true` for NON-generic Object↔Object (Checker.kt ~75575, "safe with recursive cycle detection (7.0)"), so named→named structural comparison runs. **The REMAINING named→named blocker is the `hasUnresolvedTypeParams(source/target) → return false` skip (~75541): GENERIC instantiations are still skipped because generic instantiation is incomplete.** So "the #1 named→named structural relation" framing in older notes is STALE — the real #1 blocker is **completing generic/contextual instantiation** (`hasUnresolvedTypeParams`, plus contextual arrow-param inference and generic-fn-type relation). The pins `qualify`/`errorElaboration`/`genericFunctionInference1`/`promisePermutations` all reduce to THIS one engine: each fails on a GENERIC named-type (`Container<Ref<T>>`, `<T>(x:T)=>T[]`, `then<U>`) or a namespace-nested type that resolves to errorType (which makes `canUseTypeEngine` bail at its top). Do NOT chase "open canUseTypeEngine for named→named" — it's already open; build generic instantiation.
Built-in interface types are cached at Checker init BEFORE user declarations merge via mergeSymbolTable — the cached Type.Interface freezes `baseTypes`, missing later user `interface Array<T> extends IFoo<T>` merges. Heritage must be (re)resolved via `resolveBaseTypesLazy` (eager at end of getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface + lazy when baseTypes is empty), with the interface's own type params pushed into `currentTypeParamScope`.
`resolveGenericPropertyType` re-resolves a property's declared type in the scope where it was declared, recursing through instantiated bases for inherited properties. Callers in elaboration paths MUST pair it with `getPropertyTypeForRelation` (not raw `getTypeOfSymbol`) — otherwise substituted mismatches yield no elaboration chain.
- **`implements` clauses are NOT base types for structural comparison**: `resolveBaseTypesLazy` must skip `clause.token == SyntaxKind.ImplementsKeyword` — `implements` is a structural constraint the class must satisfy, not a source of inherited members. If included, a class `C implements I` silently "inherits" I's members even when C doesn't declare them, so `propertiesRelatedTo(C, I)` trivially passes and both TS2420 (at the class declaration) and TS2344-via-constraint (at `fn<C>()` where the type param extends I) are masked. Interface `extends` clauses DO contribute members and must NOT be skipped.
- **A NAMESPACE-LOCAL `extends` base resolves through TWO coordinated sites, and touching only ONE regresses the this-member TS2339 check (round 417).** `getTypeFromBaseTypeExpression`'s bare-Identifier branch resolves the base via `lookupTypeSymbolInInferenceNamespace(name) ?: globals[name]` (a namespace-local base — `namespace M { interface Base {}; interface Derived extends Base {} }` — is NOT in `globals`; `resolveInterfaceMembers` has pushed the namespace onto `inferenceNamespaceStack`). This clears the TS2353 "inherited member looks excess" FP. **LOAD-BEARING COMPANION: once baseTypes is populated for a namespace-local base, `checkMemberAccessMissing`'s B15.1 `this.X` branch (gated `objectType.baseTypes.isNotEmpty()`) runs, and its `lookupInstanceMemberInResolvableChain` ALSO resolves bases via `globals` — a namespace-local base there returns `null` (uncertain → the `return` at the "has base types" guard swallows the diagnostic), so a genuinely-missing member is NOT reported.** So `lookupInstanceMemberInResolvableChain` takes an `enclosingNs` param (threaded from the B15.1 caller's `objectType.symbol.parent`; default null → globals-only for the 4 non-`this` callers = byte-identical) and resolves the base chain namespace-awarely. FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION: the chain returns `false` (→ emit TS2339) ONLY when it is FULLY resolvable and the member is absent everywhere; any uncertainty (sibling interface via `classNamesWithSiblingInterfaces`, index sig, `declare`, unresolvable base) propagates `null`. `genericRecursiveImplicitConstructorErrors3` (`this.isArray()` on `PullTypeSymbol extends PullSymbol`, both in `namespace TypeScript`, `isArray` declared nowhere) is the regression that pins the coupling. `hasInstanceMemberNamed` is still globals-only (not on that path — extend it symmetrically if a namespace-local-base FP surfaces there). Known gap: `classNamesWithSiblingInterfaces` is top-level-only, so a namespace-local class+interface merge is an (unexercised) FP hole; closing it precisely needs namespace-context-keyed name tracking.
- **A computed property/member name `[2]`/`["bar"]` with a STRING/NUMERIC-literal inner is a STATIC key (`[2]`→"2"), and member-NAME extraction has ≥5 INDEPENDENT sites that each drop ComputedPropertyName by default (B451)**: `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` (inline `when`), `getMemberName` (interface/class type-building — but it ALSO feeds duplicate-detection/abstract-tracking ~20 callers, so DON'T change it; use the `computedLiteralKey` fallback at the member-building call sites only), the Binder enum-member binding (`exports` keys — gates `X["bar"]` TS7015), and `classMemberNameText` (class-instance element-access lookup — the reason `z[1]`/`z[3]` resolved but `z[2]`/`z[4]` FP'd: a SEPARATE AST path from the resolved interface members, like the B419 NewExpression note). Adding computed-key support to one site silently leaves the others FP'ing; FP surface is the ~10 corpus files with a `[literal]` member name. tsc's "numeric name" rule is `isNumericLiteralName` (`"3"` yes, `"13e-1"`/`"1e3"` no).
- **A member-resolution / binder change reshuffles ~350 boundary tests via id-allocation drift — the full-suite set-diff vs a STALE `/tmp/fail_baseline.txt` is USELESS for regression detection (B451 saw 351 "fixed"/350 "regressed" for a clean +1; the documented "~25–50 boundary tests" badly understates it).** Verify instead by: (a) target passes in ISOLATION, (b) net count delta, (c) the directly-touched FP-surface tests A/B in clean-HEAD-isolation vs changed-isolation (`git stash` → batch isolation run → `git stash pop` → batch isolation run; a "regressed" test that fails in clean-HEAD isolation TOO is pre-existing, not your regression).

Static-member bifurcation: statics live in BOTH `members` and `staticMembers` (Type.kt; interfaces stay null). Any instance-vs-instance structural-comparison consumer (propertiesRelatedTo, collectMissingProperties, getMissingRequiredPropertySymbol, …) must skip target-property names that live on the static side via `getStaticMembersOfType` — otherwise inherited statics make instance shapes spuriously include them → FP TS2741. Type.Reference defers to `target.staticMembers`; value-position static access (`A.bar()`) still resolves through `members`.

### Round-140 gotchas (B283–B289)

- **tsc's two parse-error SUPPRESSION rules are now mirrored and future grammar-class checks must use them (B284/B288)**: (1) `grammarErrorOnNode` checks `hasParseDiagnostics(sourceFile)` — grammar diagnostics like TS2737 NEVER fire in a file that has ANY parser diagnostic; ours is a DRIVER post-filter (`it.code == 2737 && fileName in filesWithParseDiagnostics`, in BOTH the single-file and multi-file paths of TypeScriptCompiler.kt — a new TS2737-like grammar check needs adding to that filter). (2) `parseErrorAtPosition` skips a diagnostic whose START equals the LAST-pushed diagnostic's start — this is why `1ee` has TS1124 but NOT TS1351 (same position), and why `3a` has TS1351 but NOT TS1005. When adding parser/scanner diagnostics that can collide at one position, replicate the same-start dedup or the baseline won't match.
- **`expr.ts` arithmetic/comparison operand-category completion (round 368): the `.ts`-only arithmetic pass (`checkBinaryOperatorTypes` + `checkArithmeticInStatement` + `checkEqualityComparisonNoOverlap`) gained four coupled, FP-gated extensions — each broad, so all are narrowly gated + full-suite-verified (the `.js` skip stays load-bearing).** (1) An ANNOTATED function-body `var x: T` is recorded into the pass-local `currentLocalTypes` (the binder does NOT bind function-body vars, so `var i!: I` / `var e!: E` otherwise resolve to `any`) — GATED to a NON-UNION concrete type (`decl.type` resolves via `getTypeFromTypeNode`; a union like `number | undefined` flow-narrows and this pass has no narrowing → recording it FP'd `narrowingPastLastAssignment`). (2) `==`/`!=` between two DIFFERENT primitive comparability categories (number ⇎ string ⇎ boolean; numeric enum = number) → TS2367, reusing `comparabilityCategory` (the SAME firewall the `<`/`>` relational path relies on: null for any/bigint/object/hetero-union). Display via `ts2367CategoryDisplay`: the category name for number/string/boolean, the enum NAME for an enum. Placed early in `checkEqualityComparisonNoOverlap` (before the object/reference checks, which never fire for primitives; number-vs-bigint bails because `comparabilityCategory(bigint)==null`). (3) An `any` operand no longer SHORT-CIRCUITS strict arithmetic (`^ - * / % << >> & | ...`) — each operand is checked independently (`s^a` → TS2362 on the string LHS; `a^s` → TS2363 on the string RHS), gated so the bigint-mixing TS2365 STILL skips any operands (`leftType !== anyType && rightType !== anyType`, else `5n ^ any` FP's). The + and comparison paths KEEP the any-bail. (4) The non-wrapper-object bail is RELAXED (`arithObjectOperandBails`) for a plain INTERFACE instance (`sym` has Interface, NOT Class) or a numeric enum ONLY (`n+i`/`i^n`/`i+e` → TS2365/TS2362/TS2363) — a CLASS VALUE (`class f{}; f+=1` → tsc's TS2629 "cannot assign to a class", NOT arithmetic), a string enum, or an anonymous object still BAILS (our resolution of those is FP-prone). **A numeric enum resolves (getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker Checker.kt ~87912) to a `Type.Object` with the enum symbol, NOT a `Type.Intrinsic` with the Enum flag — so `isNumericEnumObjectType` (checks a Type.Object whose symbol has SymbolFlags.Enum and no string-valued member) teaches the four classifiers (isNumberLikeType / comparabilityCategory / isValidArithmeticOperand / typeAssignableToNumberKind) to treat it as `number`; without it every enum operand is mis-classified.** A for-IN loop var is recorded as `stringType` (scoped snapshot/restore around the loop body) so it is NOT resolved to a same-named numeric var via the block-UNAWARE file-level type map (`fileLocalTypeMaps`) — `capturedLetConstInLoop6/7`: `for (let x in []) if (x == "1")` where a later `let x = 1` leaks `number` → spurious TS2367. These arithmetic helpers are all arithmetic-pass-only, so extending them is bounded to that pass.
- **arithmetic-pass local recording extension (round 407): an un-annotated `const X = <propaccess/elemaccess/call/nonnull>` whose name SHADOWS an outer FUNCTION records into `currentLocalTypes`** (concrete primitive when `getTypeOfExpression(initializer)` resolves, else `anyType`) — the pass types a bare-identifier operand via `getTypeOfExpression`, which falls to file/global scope for an un-recorded body local, so `const length = arr.length` (a number) that shadows an imported `function length(): number` resolved to the FUNCTION → `i < length` FP'd TS2365 `number < (…) => number`. **The SHADOW gate (`getTypeOfExpression(decl.name)` is a `Type.Object` with call sigs) is LOAD-BEARING: recording EVERY primitive-typed const instead UNMASKS pre-existing narrowing FPs on the OTHER operand — `const numStatements = source.length` (number) exposes `statementOffset < numStatements` (an un-narrowed `number | undefined` param), `const max = length(sig.tp)` exposes `min < max`.** The `anyType` fallback for a genuine shadow only bails the bogus check (any operand is exempt) and cannot unmask.
- **A `x!` (NonNullExpression) arithmetic/comparison operand is stripped of nullish LOCALLY by `arithOperandType` (round 415) — do NOT move this into the global `getTypeOfExpression`.** `getTypeOfExpression(NonNullExpression)` deliberately keeps the union for `(T | undefined)!` (round 407 measured a GLOBAL nullish-strip there at net −17 but it UNMASKS M3 object-literal-vs-interface + generic-inference gaps → reverted). But a `Type.Union`'s own `.flags` carry neither the Undefined bit nor the numeric-enum bit, so `TokenFlags | undefined` fails every operand classifier (`isValidArithmeticOperand`/`typeAssignableToNumberKind`) → spurious TS2362/TS2363. `arithOperandType(e)` strips nullish members from the union WHEN `e is NonNullExpression` (tsc types `x!` as `NonNullable<typeof x>` and does arithmetic on THAT), reproducing tsc's own `templateFlags! & TokenFlags.X` / `state.affectedFilesIndex! - 1`. FP-safe: only on an explicit `!`, only REMOVES nullish (a `(string | undefined)!` still fails → still errors), local to the arithmetic pass. The residual TS2362 (`flags = flags || None; … flags & X`) is cross-statement reassignment flow-narrowing — the arithmetic pass has no narrowing (M3.4).
- **&&/ternary truthy-narrowing in the arithmetic pass (round 416, `arithTruthyNarrowedNames`) — the SIBLING of the `x!` strip above.** An `Enum | undefined` operand narrowed by an enclosing `&&` (`checkMode && checkMode & CheckMode.Inferential`) or a ternary (`length === undefined ? … : start! + length`) is a valid operand at that point, but a `Type.Union` carries no Undefined flag on itself → the classifier rejects the undefined member → spurious TS2362/TS2363/TS2365. `checkArithmeticInExpr` has a dedicated `AmpersandAmpersand` branch (run BEFORE the left-spine flatten, which would otherwise lose the narrowing scope) + a ternary branch that push the guard's non-nullish identifiers into `arithTruthyNarrowedNames` (save/restore) while walking the RIGHT / branches; `arithOperandType` strips nullish from a bare-identifier operand whose name is in that set. `collectArithTruthyNarrowableNames` (truthy: `X`, `A && B`, `X !== undefined`) + `collectArithFalsyNonNullNames` (ternary whenFalse: `X === undefined`, `!X`, `A || B` De Morgan). FP-safe BY CONSTRUCTION (a truthy operand provably has no nullish value → stripping only suppresses); the `&&` branch is safe because `checkBinaryOperatorTypes` is a no-op for `&&` itself and a `&&` is never on the left-spine of an arithmetic operator (lowest precedence).
- **CROSS-STATEMENT reassignment + enclosing-`if`-guard narrowing IS now DONE in the arithmetic pass (round 453, `arithFlowNarrowedNonNullish`) — the "arithmetic pass has no narrowing / do NOT broaden to reassignment" claims above are SUPERSEDED for a bare Identifier/PropertyAccess operand.** `arithOperandType` consults the FLOW GRAPH (via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`) so `flags = flags || None; flags & X` and `if (m !== undefined) indent += m` narrow. **LOAD-BEARING: `currentFlowGraph` is set ONLY around the narrow call (save/restore in `finally`) — setting it PASS-WIDE makes `getTypeOfExpression`'s union-receiver path flow-aware and regressed 78 tests (the `currentArithmeticFlowGraph` gotcha).** The flow-narrowed type is used ONLY when it proves non-nullish (nullish-free, non-`never`); it depends on `rhsIsDefinitelyNonNullish` classifying arithmetic/bitwise/relational/`+` BinaryExpressions + enum-member value accesses (incl. imported enums via `resolveAlias`, `receiverResolvesToRealEnum`) as non-nullish. A BARREL-imported enum (`_namespaces/ts.js`) does NOT resolve (resolveAlias can't follow the `.js` barrel) → still fires (checker.ts:6639).
- **The round-407 shadow-recording branch now covers `let`/`var`, not just `const` (round 416).** A function-body local whose name SHADOWS an outer same-named FUNCTION records into `currentLocalTypes` so a later bare-identifier arithmetic operand resolves to the shadowing local (`let min = Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY` shadows the imported `function min` → `min < args.length` was FP'ing `{ <T>(…) } < number`). A `const` records the concrete primitive (stable → can still catch a real error on the OTHER operand); a `let`/`var` records `anyType` (reassignment-proof — its INITIAL type could FP a later comparison after reassignment; `any` only ever SUPPRESSES). The SHADOW gate (`getTypeOfExpression(decl.name)` is a `Type.Object` with call sigs) is the firewall — recording a NON-shadowing local unmasks pre-existing narrowing FPs on the other operand.
- **A BRANDED number `number & { __brand }` IS a valid arithmetic operand (round 407)** — an intersection is a subtype of each member, so `number & X` is assignable to `number`. The operand classifiers (`isNumberLikeType`/`isBigIntLikeType`/`isStringLikeType` + the B283 `typeAssignableTo*Kind`) now treat a `Type.Intersection` as number-/bigint-/string-like iff ANY constituent is (they already did Union/TypeParam). Without it, tsc's own `type IncrementalBuildInfoFileId = number & {…}` FP'd TS2362 on `fileId - 1`.
- **Accessor-pair visibility is per-DIRECTION (B289, `checkDivergentAccessorVisibility`)**: the SETTER's modifier governs an assignment-target access (including compound assigns and `++`/`--` — tsc checks ONLY the setter there, even though `+=` also reads), the GETTER's governs reads; exactly ONE diagnostic per access at the property name. Uniform-PRIVATE pairs must stay excluded — the pre-existing TS2341 paths own them and the walker would double-emit; uniform-protected and all divergent combos have no other emitter.

### Round-139 gotchas (B277–B282)

- **A NAMESPACE-NESTED interface/class member's param/return ANNOTATIONS resolve through TWO separate sites, BOTH needing the containing namespace pushed (B280)**: `namespace O { class A; interface I { g(a: A): C } }` — `resolveInterfaceMembers` (wrapped: pushes via `pushInferenceNamespaceFor(type.symbol)`) builds the member TABLE, but the member's TYPE is resolved lazily in `getTypeOfSymbol`'s **MethodDeclaration branch** of `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`, which walks `symbol.parent` to the nearest Module ancestor and pushes it. Without the second push, A/C resolve to errorType, the method's overload signatures carry errorType params/returns (DISPLAYED 'any' per B58.1 — looks like anyType in markers!), and overload-return selection silently dies (overload1). `getTypeOfSymbol` CACHES by symbol.id, so the resolution context at FIRST touch wins.
The comma/nullish-predicates ordering contracts are STRUCTURAL since round 541 (both ride the spine): comma TS2695 anchors dispatch at node ENTERS (pre-order = outer-before-inner), np `??` anchors at BinaryExpression LEAVES (tsc post-order = inner-before-outer, right subtree first), while/do truthiness at the CONDITION node's leave — same-position comma-before-np holds because enters precede leaves; do NOT move either anchor to the other phase. The classifiers `nullishSemanticsOf`/`truthySemanticsOf` are EXACT mirrors of tsc's getSyntacticNullishnessSemantics/getSyntacticTruthySemantics — change them only against the tsc source.
- **Method-call TS2554 too-many-args MUST exclude embedded-lib signatures (B279)**: the embedded lib simplifies optional params away (`toLocaleString(locales?, options?)` → `toLocaleString()`, `forEach(cb, thisArg?)` → `forEach(cb)`), so `sig.declaration in builtinLibMemberDecls` → return. Too-FEW is safe unguarded (a simplified lib sig never RAISES minArgumentCount). The multi-sig overload-arity branch (TS2554 with cross-overload range, before checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads) has the same lib gate; its too-few TS6210 points at params[argCount] of the overload with the SMALLEST minArgumentCount (overload1's 17:11 'n').
- **`undefined!`/`null!` is `never` in BOTH expression-typing systems (B282)**: `getTypeOfExpression`'s NonNullExpression branch (nullish intrinsic → neverType) AND the string-based `inferSimpleExprType` (inner "undefined"/"null" → null = check-skipped). Fixing only one leaves `return undefined!` FP'ing TS2322 through the OTHER path (the varTypes-based return check at the `return` keyword was the actual emitter — checkReturnAssignability's string fallback).

### Checker gotchas

- **The INV.4 check spine (`checkSpine`, round 514) is THE walk for migrated per-node checks (`spineEnterNode`/`spineLeaveNode` `when` dispatch; always-on since batch 1) — a NEW handler carries its OWN option/file-kind gates (there is NO file-level dts/js skip; per-run gates are fields computed at checkSpine entry, pre-`init` declared).** Prefer a spine handler over a new full-tree pass; a handler sees ALL nodes (a hand-walk's under-visits become visits — decide widen-vs-gate per the emission-direction rule; a module-scope-only old walk reproduces as the module-chain parent-walk gate, cf. spineCheckGlobalAugmentation; a COLLECT-then-SCAN walker migrates as spine collection + BUFFERED positions resolved at file END — per-file state cleared in checkSpine's loop, cf. spineCollectObjLitVar/spineResolveDeferredIterationChecks, preserving use-before-decl matching with no prepass walk; a per-PARAMETER rule keyed on the owning construct dispatches on `param.parent` kind, cf. spineCheckRestParam's value-vs-type-position split; a walker threading MULTIPLE flags with different reset rules (ambient + atTopLevel) migrates as ONE parent-chain walk where the INNERMOST flag-deciding ancestor wins per flag and any non-descended ancestor kind returns null = the old no-visit, cf. spineDupModContext/spineAmbientInitContext; a per-statement-LIST stateful walker — fresh binding maps per nested block, accumulated in statement order — migrates as a preceding-sibling scan of the parent list AT THE CONSUMER node, single-statement positions degrading to `listOf(stmt)`, cf. spineSwitchSubjectBinding/spineSiblingStatements; a walker whose STATEMENT walk stops at ambient decl bodies but whose EXPRESSION walk resets ambient unconditionally needs BOTH rules in one parent-chain walk — own-`declare` on a passed decl-body owner returns null, a declare-module found ABOVE a passed decl-body returns null via a `passedDeclBody` flag, and expression-fn boundaries (arrow/fn-expr/class-EXPR-member/objlit-method bodies) CLEAR that flag while deciding ambient=false, cf. spineAmbientImplContext — forgetting the clear wrongly nulls a fn nested arrow-deep inside a declare-module; a walker that SKIPS a subtree by construction-context — the destructuring-assignment-LHS pattern — migrates as a came-from-child parent climb through pattern-position parents only (a PropertyAssignment continues the climb ONLY when the child is its INITIALIZER; any other parent kind terminates → checked), answering skip iff a `=` BinaryExpression is reached with the climbed child as its LEFT, cf. spineObjLitInDestructuringLhs; a walker threading NEAREST-BOUNDARY flags (isAsync + the related-info FuncRef) migrates as a full parent-chain walk where the FIRST function-like boundary decides the flags but EVERY step up to the SourceFile must still be an old-walked position — deciding flags at the first boundary and STOPPING wrongly reaches e.g. an arrow nested in a parameter default, cf. spineAwaitCtx; a TYPING walker with per-scope stacks (INV.4(d), cf. spineUncalledWithScopes round 530) REBUILDS them pull-based at each emission from the anchor's ancestor chain with per-owner memoized LAZY levels — sound only when the level collection is position-independent and consults only OUTER levels — and save-set-restores its ambient state (currentFlowGraph/currentCheckFileName) around EACH dispatch, never walk-wide (the currentArithmeticFlowGraph 78-test hazard); an emission that legacy-ran INSIDE the owner's scope push anchors the rebuild at the BODY, not the owner (arrow expr-body truthiness); an ORDER-DEPENDENT recording pass (statement-ordered currentLocalTypes recordings that deliberately LEAK across blocks/if-branches/accessor bodies — checkArithmeticOperandTypes, round 531) migrates PUSH-based instead: ONE LIFO of (child-node, saved-value) frames maintained by per-node enter/leave hooks, edge frames keyed on the CHILD and popped at its leave AFTER the node's own emissions, shared fields swapped to pass-private structures only inside the per-emission install; its legacy left-spine flatten reproduces as chain-root LEAVE emission (innermost-first; root = non-`&&`/`=`-objlit and not left-ABSORBED — the flatten descends left unconditionally, so any left-edge ancestor with a non-special operator absorbs the node and its special branch never runs); a pass migrating from AFTER the three giants must be SLOT-MOVE A/B'd first (move the intact pass to the spine slot, listAll ×8) — the arithmetic pass coupled BOTH ways: its currentParamBindingNames leak into the giants (now pass-private), and it CONSUMED the TS2322 walk's namespace-level currentLocalTypes recording residue — only the CORPUS caught that (qualify.ts; zero instances on the 8 tsc profiles), reproduced as the pass's own ModuleBlock-gated identifier-init chain recording — so a slot-moved pass needs the corpus suite run on the slot-move experiment too, not just the profile A/B; a DOWNWARD-CONTEXT-THREADING walker (explicit context params passed through an expression recursion — checkImplicitAnyParameters, round 532) migrates as ONE push-maintained ctx value with frames defined at EXACTLY the edges the legacy recursion passed arguments over — the load-bearing rule: every REACHED expression-position edge must DEFINE the ctx (even to null), or the parent ctx silently LEAKS where the legacy call passed defaults; one-level special states (call-args/objlit-enrichment/destructure-defaults) ride KINDED carrier values that only the immediate child edges read, a per-operand-uniform binary left-spine loop dissolves into per-edge operator rules (no chain-root machinery — unlike arith, whose EMISSIONS were chain-rooted), and statement-threaded flags (returnCtxAnnotation/inAmbientContext) pull-derive from parent chains because the reach classifier already guarantees the path shape; a STATELESS multi-COMPONENT pass (tree walk + per-file top-level scans — checkDuplicateIdentifiers, round 533) migrates as a boolean reach classifier + anchor dispatch running the RETAINED bounded leaf utilities, its file-level scans riding checkSpine's loop at file enter/leave in the legacy within-file order — and must REPRODUCE THE LEGACY PASS-AMBIENT: the old slot ran with `currentFileLocals = null` while checkSpine sets it per file, so every migrated scan consulting it needs a null install (checkClassNamespacePrototypeConflict's `currentFileLocals ?: globals` fallback silently changes meaning otherwise); a per-statement-LIST ordered walker with DOWNWARD context (checkDefiniteAssignment, round 534) migrates as CORE FRAMES pushed at list-owner enters (per-statement steps at direct-child statement enters — `parent === top.owner`, no classifier) + a multi-state reach classifier whose context-flavored statuses READ the top frame's per-statement snapshot at spawn time — sound ONLY while context-preserving edges never cross another frame spawn (here: every core-spawning Block/ModuleBlock drops or consumes the leak); a sibling pass that DEDUPS one-directionally against the migrating pass's emitted diagnostics (checkDefiniteAssignmentViaFlowGraph scans `diagnostics` for TS2454 starts) must MOVE WITH it to keep emitter-order (siblings re-dispatched right after the spine); a walker whose recursion carries a DEPTH CAP (checkArgumentCounts' argCountDepth ≤200, round 535) migrates as an INT-valued reach classifier — the legacy counter reproduced per edge (expression-position edges +1 then capped; a binary RIGHT operand that is itself a binary is ABSORBED at the parent's depth = the old right-spine loop, so right-leaning chains never consume depth while left-nested ones prune); a MAP-valued downward context that is a pure function of the ancestor chain (whole-list overlays, never a positional prefix) rebuilds PULL-based via per-list-owner memoized levels, and a pull rebuild that RE-ENTERS itself through its own memoized levels must reuse its shared ascent buffer MARK-based, never clear()-based (the clear silently drops the outer frame's recorded edges — the for-of loop-shadow pin caught it); a walker whose per-statement work is a RETAINED bounded sub-walk that itself recurses some statement kinds (checkUBDForwardRefs descends if/labeled) anchors ONLY at DIRECT statements of activated lists — anchoring at nested positions double-emits (round 536); a pass whose emitter CO-EMITS a second code consumed by a later pass's dedup scan (emitTS2448's TS2454 vs checkDefiniteAssignmentViaFlowGraph) changes dedup visibility when it moves before that pass — slot-move pre-gate with the CORPUS, not just listAll;
