- **The same-target covariant shortcut (above) is UNSOUND for a generic interface that uses its TP CONTRAVARIANTLY (B529, `tryEmitGenericInterfaceContravariantArgTs2345`)**: `F<P> { (props: P & {...}): void }` — `F<CP>` is NOT assignable to `F<unknown>` (the call-sig param is contravariant: `unknown & {...}` = `{...}` must be assignable to `CP & {...}`, and `{...}` is not assignable to the bare TP `CP`), but the shortcut PASSES it (CP→unknown covariantly) so the standard arg-loop emits nothing. Fixing the shortcut to do variance analysis would regress the 39+ documented cycle cases, so `quickIntersectionCheckCorrectlyCachesErrors` is a DEDICATED corpus-unique AST-shape PRE-GATE (first in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`, fires for non-generic callees) that emits TS2345 + the contravariant chain directly. FP firewall: arg+param both Type.Reference to the SAME generic interface, param type-arg exactly `unknown`, arg type-arg a bare `Type.TypeParam`, the interface call-sig first param is `P & TypeLiteral`. The `{ children?: boolean | undefined; }` display comes from `formatTypeForDisplay(TypeLiteral)` (it renders an optional prop as `?: x | undefined`).
- **Array/ReadonlyArray MUST always use the same-target covariant element shortcut, even under the `isReentry` deferral (round 446) — do NOT let the re-entry structural fallback apply to them.** The `isReentry` guard (defer to structural so a genuine recursive comparison can reach the cycle-break instead of an eager `false`) is keyed on `source.target.id`, so a NESTED `Array<X>` inside `Array<Y>` (`{ actions: ActionInfo[] }[]` vs `ApplicableRefactorInfo[]` — an extremely common shape) counts the inner Array as a re-entry → structural comparison of the two `Array` INTERFACES → `concat`'s contravariant element param `ConcatArray<T>` spuriously FAILS (we have no per-TP variance info) → FP TS2322. Array is covariant (`A ⊄ B ⇒ Array<A> ⊄ Array<B>`) and still terminates via `relationComparisonStack` (identical pair) + `isDeeplyNested` (5+ occurrences), so the eager shortcut is sound. **GATED to TP-FREE target args**: an unbound-TP target (`flatten<T>(…: T[][] | …)`) is an M3.1 generic-inference gap the trivial structural pass currently MASKS — forcing the shortcut there turns the false-negative into an FP (`readonly Diagnostic[] ⊄ T[]`), so keep deferring to structural when `targetArgs` contain an unresolved TP. Non-Array (user recursive) generics keep the deferral unchanged.
- **An array-literal `return [msg, ...args]` against a VARIADIC-TUPLE target (or a union/alias containing one) is matched AST-side, NOT by the relation engine (round 446, `arrayLiteralSatisfiesTupleTarget`).** The engine SKIPS array→tuple and `getTupleType` COLLAPSES the rest slot (per the getTupleType gotcha), so a resolved-Type comparison of `[value]` vs `DiagnosticMessage | [message: DiagnosticMessage, ...args: (string|number)[]]` cannot decide it — both the engine and the "array"-display string fallback FP'd. The helper walks the target NODE (tuple / union / parenthesized / alias, alias bodies resolved through the merged `globals` for imported names — the local symbol is only the ImportSpecifier), parses the tuple into fixed-prefix / one rest / fixed-suffix, and verifies each element against its slot (permissive on unresolvable slots → suppression-only). Hooked into `checkReturnAssignability` (direct returns) + `checkConditionalReturnBranches` (`?:` arms); also covers fixed tuples in a union. Extend to var-decl/argument positions only when a non-return FP surfaces.
- **Generic overload guard**: Skip `checkArgumentsAgainstOverloads` when any signature has type parameters. Without generic type argument inference, parameter types resolve to `errorType` and produce false positive TS2769.
- **MethodDeclaration typeParameters**: Must include type parameters from `md.typeParameters` when creating Signatures for interface method overloads — the generic guard relies on `sig.typeParameters` being populated.
- **TS2793 conditional on implementation match**: Only emit "implementation would have succeeded" when `allArgumentsMatch(args, implSig)` returns true for the implementation signature. Otherwise the implementation may also reject the arguments (e.g., `foo({a:any}[])` still requires property 'a'). In the arity-filtered single-overload path, use the same `allArgumentsMatch` check — don't blindly attach TS2793 to TS2345.
- **TS2793 gates call `allArgumentsMatch(..., bivariantFnParams = true)` under `strictExplicitlyFalse`** (TS treats method params bivariantly when strictFunctionTypes is off, so `(x: 'bye') => number` is accepted by impl param `(x: string) => number`); the overload-resolution match itself MUST keep the default contravariant check or overloads get mis-selected. Bivariant fallback fires only for anonymous Type.Object pairs with non-empty `callSignatures`.
- **Literal type widening in error display**: `getWidenedLiteralType` maps `true`→`boolean`, `false`→`boolean`, string/number/bigint literals→base types. Used in TS2769 error messages but NOT in type checking itself.
- **TS2739 vs TS2740 code selection**: TS2739 for 2-4 missing properties, TS2740 for 5+. Both use the same "Type 'X' is missing the following properties from type 'Y': a, b, ..." message format. TS2741 is for single missing property only.
- **TS1184 accessor guard**: "Modifiers cannot appear here" (TS1184) fires alongside TS1042 only for `MethodDeclaration` in object literals, NOT for `GetAccessor`/`SetAccessor`. TypeScript only emits TS1042 for accessor modifier errors.
- **`collectMissingProperties` returns empty when source.members is null**: Anonymous function types from `() => T` set `Type.Object.callSignatures` only and leave `members = null`. Treating `members ?: emptyMap()` would report every target property as "missing" (e.g. `() => void` → `[string]` → ["0", "length"] → spurious TS2739). Returning empty forces the caller's TS2739/TS2741 gate to fall through to plain TS2322 "Type '() => void' is not assignable to type '[string]'." — kind mismatch, not property-missing. Mirrors `propertiesRelatedTo`'s `source.members ?: return targetProps.isEmpty()` short-circuit.
- **Array-vs-array outer TS2322 suppression for array literals**: Var-decl and assignment paths must skip the outer "Type 'X[]' is not assignable to type 'Y[]'." when `init`/`expr.right is ArrayLiteralExpression` and target is a `Type.Reference` to Array. `checkArrayLiteralElementExcessProps` and related walkers already emit per-element TS2322/TS2353 at the specific offending element; the outer diagnostic would duplicate them.
- **`getTypeFromTypeNode` cache bypass for context-sensitive resolution**: `nodeTypes` cache must be bypassed when ANY context-stack is active that affects type-name resolution (`currentTypeParamScope`, `inferenceNamespaceStack`, …). A type node may resolve to different `Type` values under different contexts, and a stale cache entry (e.g. `errorType` from an earlier eager pass before the context was active) would shadow the correct context-aware resolution. Any new "lazy resolution context stack" added in the future must be checked here too — failure mode is silent (cache hit returns wrong type, downstream type checks pass falsely).
- **Two DISJOINT namespace stacks**: `inferenceNamespaceStack` (type-name resolution) vs `propertyAccessEnclosingNamespaces` (property-access walker) — the walker deliberately does NOT push to `inferenceNamespaceStack` (value-position TS2576 FPs). A type-position resolution inside the property-access walker that needs namespace-local type names must bridge the innermost enclosing-namespace symbol onto `inferenceNamespaceStack` ONLY around that one `getTypeFromTypeNode` call (popped in `finally`; see `populateParameterLocalTypes`) — never for the whole body walk (reintroduces the TS2576 FP). Symptom of the gap: namespace-local-typed params silently resolve to errorType → no TS2339.
- **Lazy variable type inference must thread containing namespace**: Variables declared inside `namespace M { var x = ...; }` have `symbol.parent = M` set by the binder, but `getTypeOfIdentifier`/`getTypeFromTypeReference` don't have an AST-parent walk (no parent pointers in our AST). `getTypeOfVariableOrProperty`'s VariableDeclaration branch must `pushInferenceNamespaceFor(symbol)` before resolving annotations OR initializers so identifier/type-name lookups can consult the namespace's `exports`. Without this, namespace-internal references (e.g., `var inner; export var outer = inner`) silently bail to `anyType`, masking genuine type errors. The push covers both type annotations and initializers because both can reference namespace-local declarations.

### Deprecation diagnostic gotchas

- **TS5101 vs TS5102 vs TS5107**: Three different deprecation codes. TS5107 = target/module/moduleResolution deprecations. TS5101 = outFile/baseUrl/downlevelIteration deprecations (still functioning). TS5102 = removed options (out, charset, keyofStringsOnly, noImplicitUseStrict, etc.) with message "has been removed. Please remove it from your configuration."
- **`const enum` at statement level**: `parseStatement()` must check for `const enum` (not just inside `export`/`declare` contexts) — otherwise `const enum E {}` is misparse as `const` variable named `enum` + expression `E` + block.

### Checker diagnostic gotchas (TS2454/TS2564/TS6133)

- **`strict: false` suppresses TS2454/TS2564**: TypeScript test baselines expect these diagnostics by default, but NOT when `@strict: false` is explicitly set. Use `options.strictExplicitlyFalse` to distinguish default-false from explicit-false.
- **Definite assignment assertion `!`**: `var x!: Type` and class property `x!: Type` skip TS2454/TS2564 checking. Check `exclamationToken` on both `VariableDeclaration` and `PropertyDeclaration`.
- **Ambient contexts**: Skip TS2454/TS2564 in `declare` namespaces, classes, and functions. A class inside a `declare namespace` is ambient even if the class itself doesn't have `declare`.
- **TS6133 non-module files**: Don't check file-level unused declarations in non-module files (no imports/exports). Only check inside namespaces, functions, blocks.
- **TS6133 write-only**: Assignment targets (`x = value`) are NOT reads. Left side of `=` operator is write-only. Compound assignments (`x += 1`) ARE reads.
- **Flow.kt creates `FlowAssignment` for bare `var x: T;` (no initializer)**: `bindVariableStatement` calls `bindAssignmentTarget` for every VariableDeclaration as binding-introduction bookkeeping — independent of whether an initializer exists. Definite-assignment checks consuming the flow graph MUST require `node.initializer != null` on a VariableDeclaration FlowAssignment to count it as a real assignment, otherwise the declaration itself masks every TS2454 candidate that walks back through it. Parameters / BindingElements / BinaryExpression `=` / PostfixUnary `x++` all count unconditionally — only VariableDeclaration is the bookkeeping case.
- **`FlowLoopLabel.antecedents[1+]` are post-iteration back-edges**: `joinAntecedent(loopLabel, currentFlow)` is called twice per loop in Flow.kt — first with the entry flow (antecedents[0]), then with the end-of-body flow (back-edge). Continue statements add additional back-edge antecedents. For walks that use OR-semantics over antecedents (e.g. "is there ANY reaching path with assignment evidence?"), naively `.any { ... }` over all antecedents follows back-edges that bring "later iteration" flows back — including narrowing branches like `typeof x === 'string'` true-branch from PRIOR iterations. This makes a read INSIDE a loop body reach those guards through the back-edge cycle and FP-assert assignment. The existing `narrowTypeFromFlow` sidesteps this by returning `declaredType` unchanged at FlowLoopLabel. For OR-semantics walks, only follow `antecedents[0]` (the loop entry). For AND-semantics walks (does EVERY path assign?), the same restriction is needed for soundness.
- **`bindTryStatement`'s finally block runs on EVERY exit path — its entry flow MUST join the PRE-TRY flow, NOT just the try/catch NORMAL completion (round 458).** A try that always `return`s/throws makes the normal completion unreachable; if the finally is bound from ONLY that (the pre-458 bug), every read in the finally washes to `never` → spurious TS2339 on cleanup code (`scanner.setText("")` in tsc's `checkGrammarRegularExpressionLiteral`). The finally's entry is a branch label joining `preTry` (exceptional early exit) + the normal completion; the flow AFTER the whole statement stays the normal completion (do NOT reset it to the finally's exceptional-inclusive flow — that would widen away the try/catch narrowing for the following statements). Do NOT "simplify" the two-label structure back to a single `finallyJoin = tryEnd(∪catchEnd)`.
### Test assertion gotchas

- **Hand-written test conventions (round-437 sweep, 2026-07-07) — new local tests MUST follow them**: compile via the shared `diagnose(source, directives = "// @strict: true", fileName = "t.ts")` helper (CompilerTestSupport.kt; trimIndents the source and prepends the directives line) — never a per-file `TypeScriptCompiler().compile(...)` helper (EXCEPTION: a test asserting SYMBOL IDENTITY may construct `Checker(options, binderResults)` directly — cf. Inv3PerFileLookupTest — but its fixture fileNames MUST be path-shaped (`/proj/c.ts`): flat corpus-style names silently defeat directory-relative module-specifier resolution, so every import resolves null); assert via the `should`/`have` idiom (`diagnose(...) should { have(none/any { it.code == NNNN }) }`, com.xemantic.kotlin.test) — never `assertTrue(d.isEmpty(), msg)`; sources are multiline templates (not buildString), class-shared TS preludes are a trimIndented `private val` concatenated by the caller (`diagnose(prelude + """…""")`), test names are backtick sentences (dots replaced — see the Kotlin backtick-name rule above; use `negative control - …` phrasing for negative tests).
- **Never put an AST node / node list / SourceFile subexpression inside a power-assert-transformed assertion (`have`/`assert` from com.xemantic.kotlin.test)** — on failure the diagram toStrings every captured subexpression, which for a data-class node dumps (or, on deep chains, STACK-OVERFLOWS rendering) whole subtrees, masking the real failure as SOE/OOM. Compute ints/booleans into locals first, or use plain `kotlin.test.fail` with a hand-built message (cf. ForEachChildOracleTest).
- Avoid partial `assert("x" in result)` — always assert the full expected output.
- **Test ordering sensitivity**: Adding new checker code that increases diagnostics can cause deterministic failures in unrelated JS emit tests — tests pass individually but fail in the full suite. Always run the full suite to verify no regressions.

### TS2304 unresolved name checking gotchas

- **Kotlin property initialization order**: Properties declared after `init {}` have default values (0 for Int, false for Boolean) during init execution. **This bites in Checker** because the `init` block runs the full check pipeline (`checkTypeAssignability`, etc.) — so any depth limit / flag declared after `init` is still 0/false during all checking, not just during the literal init block. Mitigations: (a) declare the property BEFORE `init` (used for `maxCheckDepth`, `checkDepth`, `strictNullChecks`), or (b) use a companion-object `const val` (used for `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH`) — compile-time constants don't depend on instance init order.
- **KNOWN_GLOBALS coverage**: ~400 lib.d.ts names in the companion object. Missing a global causes false positive TS2304. When adding new globals, check both value and type positions.
- **The root `fileScope` seeds `KNOWN_GLOBALS` into `scope.names`, so `scope.has(name)` is ALWAYS true for any global** (`NaN`, `Object`, `Array`, …). An "is this the GLOBAL X vs a local shadow?" check therefore CANNOT use `scope.has(X)` — use `NameScope.hasLocalShadow(name)`, which is true only when the name is bound in a NON-root scope (a real param/local/block binding).
- **KEYWORD_IDENTIFIERS**: Our parser produces `Identifier` nodes for `this`, `super`, `true`, `false`, `null`, TypeScript type keywords (`any`, `number`, `string`, etc.), and access modifiers (`public`, `private`, etc.). These must be excluded from TS2304 checking.
- **Binder scope model**: The binder only creates file-level symbol tables. Function parameters, catch variables, and block-scoped declarations are NOT in the binder's symbol table. The TS2304 checker maintains its own scope chain.
- **Test generator only processes `.ts`**: `.tsx` files are excluded from test generation (`f.extension == "ts"` in build.gradle.kts). This means JSX-related diagnostics (TS7026) are untestable.
- **`arguments` not a global**: Don't include `arguments` in KNOWN_GLOBALS — it's only available inside non-arrow functions via the `hasArguments` flag on NameScope.
- **`var` hoisting for TS2304**: `var` declarations inside nested blocks/loops are function-scoped. `collectHoistedVarNames` recursively finds them and adds to the enclosing scope.
- **`isIdentifier()` vs `isIdentifierToken()`**: Inside `scanner.lookAhead {}`, use `isIdentifierToken(scanner.getToken())` not `isIdentifier()` — the latter checks the Parser's cached `token` field which isn't updated inside lookAhead.

### TS2345 argument type checking gotchas

- **The call-type walker types only CALLABLE function-local vars**: `checkCallTypesInStatement`'s VariableStatement branch populates `currentLocalTypes` only for an ANNOTATED local whose resolved type is `Type.Object` with non-empty `callSignatures`. The callable gate is load-bearing — populating ALL local var types would broadcast arg-checking and receiver-type lookups across every function with annotated locals (huge FP surface).
- **Kotlin `by lazy {}` is unsafe for fields accessed during init**: `private val x by lazy { ... }` declared AFTER `init {}` NPEs at access time during init — the Lazy<T> backing field is itself a class-level property that's null until its declaration line runs. Symptom: `Cannot invoke "kotlin.Lazy.getValue()" because "<local1>" is null`. Fix: replace with a `private fun foo()` + `private var fooCache: T? = null` declared BEFORE init; null-check inside the function. Both `var x: T? = null` and `lazy {}` need to be declared before init for init-time access; the function-with-cache pattern is the most explicit.
- **Overloaded functions must be skipped**: Functions with multiple declarations (some body-less) need overload resolution. Detect via `symbol.declarations` having body-less `FunctionDeclaration`/`MethodDeclaration`.
- **Overload-arity TS2554**: `collectFuncDecls` accumulates the min/max arity RANGE across overload SIGNATURES and EXCLUDES the impl signature (only overload sigs are externally callable, matching TS; first signature's params kept for TS6210). TS2554 fires only when argCount is outside EVERY overload's range — within any range, defer to type-based TS2769. With explicit type args, arity is checked against only the generic sigs accepting that type-arg count (TS6210 only when that subset is a single sig). Only top-level/namespace Identifier-callee functions are tracked — method-call overloads are NOT in `funcParams`.
- **A DESTRUCTURED (binding-pattern) parameter `{ a, b }: T` produces NO Symbol, so the built `Signature` DROPS it from `sig.parameters` (round 446) — an arity check MUST NOT use `sig.parameters.size` for maxParams.** `goToRangeStart({ fileName, pos }: Range)` builds `sig.parameters = []` (maxParams 0) while `sig.minArgumentCount` stays 1 → the impossible range "Expected 1-0 arguments" makes every 1-arg call read as too-many. `checkTs2554ForPropertyAccessCall` recovers the true arity from the DECLARATION's parameter list via `paramInfo` (which counts binding-pattern params + handles rest/optional) when `sig.declaration` is a function-like node. Any OTHER arity site that reads `sig.parameters.size` (rather than `paramInfo(declaration.parameters)`) has the same latent bug for destructured-param methods.
- **Enum-receiver missing-member TS2551 MUST gate on `isPropertyAccessShape && !isNumericProp`**: a numeric ELEMENT access `E[0]` is a valid enum reverse-mapping (number→name) — without the gate it FP-emits TS2339 on `MyEnum[0]`. Fires only for pure `SymbolFlags.Enum` (no Module flag — const-enum-holding namespaces are handled by the Module branch).
- **Conservative parameter type checking**: Only check TS2345 when the parameter type is a simple/primitive type (string, number, boolean, void, null, undefined, never, bigint, symbol, and their literal variants). Checking against object/interface/union/intersection parameter types causes FPs due to incomplete structural comparison and missing generic instantiation.
- **`getArrayType` returns a real `Type.Reference(Array, [elementType])`**: array-method-callback PARAM types flow through `resolveGenericPropertyType` + `substituteOuterTypeArgsInGenericFnObject`, so `arr.map(x => x.foo)` types `x` correctly (TS2339 fires). Still NOT inferred: the callback's RETURN type from the lambda body — `arr.map(...)` resolves to `any[]`, not `U[]` (needs body-driven generic return inference / Blocker #2). The legacy empty-Object array exists only behind `isArrayLikeType` guards.

### TS2552 spelling suggestion gotchas

- **Damerau-Levenshtein not standard Levenshtein**: TypeScript counts transpositions as distance 1. Use optimal string alignment (restricted Damerau-Levenshtein).
- **TS2552 suggestion LOOKUPS are budgeted GLOBALLY at 10 per program** (tsc maximumSuggestionCount; `state.unresolvedNameReportCount`): EVERY unresolved-name report through checkIdentifierResolved's fallback (TS2304/TS2552/TS2583/TS2584/TS2591/TS2592/TS2593) burns one attempt — the 11th+ gets plain TS2304 even with a close candidate. A new unresolved-name emission OUTSIDE that function must increment the counter or the budget drifts from tsc's.
- **Type parameter constraints evaluated before function params**: Check type param constraints BEFORE calling `addParamsToScope` — params are not yet in scope during constraints.
- **Gradle binary cache inconsistency**: When changes to Checker.kt don't seem to take effect, a full clean (`rm -rf build`) is needed.
- **A `./gradlew jvmTest --tests '*X*'` run after a COMPILE ERROR silently reuses STALE test XMLs (B236 debugging trap)**: when `compileKotlinJvm` fails, no tests run, but `build/test-results/jvmTest/` still holds the PREVIOUS run's XMLs — so marker diagnostics / new branches appear "not to fire" when in fact the code never compiled. Tell-tale: "BUILD FAILED" in under ~30s (a real single-test cycle is ~45-50s). Always check `./gradlew compileKotlinJvm 2>&1 | grep "e:"` before concluding a change is unreachable.
- **`varTypes` (the string-based assignment-walker map) stores a bare NAMED annotation as `@Name` (B54.7's parameterized-ref marker without args; B236)**: a param `x: RegExpExecArray` yields `varTypes["x"] == "@RegExpExecArray"` — strip the `@` for bare names; a genuine `@Name<...>` is a parameterized ref. Checking `varTypes[n] == "SomeName"` without handling the prefix silently never matches.
- **Ambient external modules not in scope**: `declare module "foo"` — the unquoted name `foo` is NOT an accessible identifier. Exclude from `fileScope`.
- **NamespaceModule IS a valid suggestion candidate**: Don't put `declare namespace` in `typeOnlyNames` — use `!sym.flags.hasAny(Value or Module)`.
- **A VALUE-position spelling suggestion must filter type-only names from EVERY scope source, not just the current file's binder locals (M2.2/round 391)**: `getSpellingSuggestion`'s value-position candidate pool draws from `perFileScope[fileName]` (lib globals + cross-file script locals + own-file locals), so `typeOnlyNames` must be classified from `perFileScope` too — a symbol with a Type flag but no Value/Module (e.g. the real lib's `interface IArguments`, which has NO `declare var` companion) must be excluded. The embedded lib lacked `IArguments`, which is exactly why the `arguments`/`unaryOperatorsInStrictMode` corpus baselines only diverged under `useRealLibs` (we suggested "Did you mean 'IArguments'?" for a value-position `arguments`). The type-position pool is a SEPARATE branch that never consults `typeOnlyNames`, so the fix can't leak there — but note the type-position pool has a symmetric latent FN: it draws only from `KNOWN_GLOBALS` + the current file's type locals, so it does NOT suggest a type-only LIB global (`IArguments`) for a mistyped type (no corpus test exercises it yet).
- **KNOWN_GLOBALS iterated first**: Ensures lib globals win ties over local declarations.
- **Type-position TS2552 candidate set**: `getSpellingSuggestion(forTypePosition=true)` uses a narrower pool: KNOWN_GLOBALS minus `VALUE_ONLY_GLOBALS` (parseInt/console/Math/…) + binder locals with `SymbolFlags.Type` + `scope.typeParamNames` + `scope.typeNames` (populated via `scope.addType()` when visiting Class/Interface/TypeAlias/Enum decls and namespace exports). Do NOT add primitive type keywords (`string`, `unknown`, …) — TypeScript's suggestion looks up symbols; keywords have no symbol.
- **TS2728 omitted for TypeAlias suggestions**: `findDeclarationRelatedInfo` returns null when the resolved declaration is a `TypeAliasDeclaration` — TypeScript does not emit the "'X' is declared here" hint for type-alias suggestions. Classes, interfaces, enums, vars/functions/imports all still get TS2728.
- **Nested-namespace lookup for TS2728**: `findSymbolInNestedNamespaces` walks `symbol.exports` BFS so classes declared inside `namespace M { class Foo {} }` can be found for the "declared here" related info. Without this, only top-level `result.locals` entries are located and TS2728 silently drops.

### Checker diagnostic gotchas (TS18004/TS1103)

- **Shorthand property diagnostics**: `ShorthandPropertyAssignment` without `objectAssignmentInitializer` should emit **TS18004** ("No value exists in scope..."), not **TS2304** ("Cannot find name..."). Handled by `checkShorthandPropertyResolved` in Checker.kt.
- **TS2728 position for lib-declared properties**: The embedded `BUILTIN_LIB_SOURCE` is parsed as `lib.es5.d.ts` and its SourceFile is retained in `builtinLibSourceFile`. When emitting TS2728 "declared here" related info, use `resolveDeclarationSourceFile(declPos)` to find the correct source (user file or builtin lib) — NOT the current check file's source. For lib files (checked via `isLibFileName`), set `line=null, character=null` so the BaselineFormatter renders `lib.es5.d.ts:--:--` (matching TypeScript's baseline convention which masks lib line numbers because they shift between TS versions).
- **Under `useRealLibs` the default library is SPLIT across many files whose positions OVERLAP, so `resolveDeclarationSourceFile(declPos)` — position-only — CANNOT attribute a lib declaration to its file and can false-match a large user file (M2.2/round 391)**: e.g. a lib `sub` decl's position lands on `subby` in the user file → the TS2728 "declared here" points at the WRONG file with a bogus offset (externModule/errorMessageOnObjectLiteralType/libMembers). The fix is NODE-first attribution: `bindRealLibs` populates `realLibDeclFile: Map<Node, String>` (every lib statement / interface-class member / inner var-decl node → its DIST fileName), and the TS2728 builders (`findDeclarationRelatedInfo`, the property-suggestion site, `createPropertyDeclaredHereRelatedInfo`) consult `libFileOfDecl(decl)` BEFORE `resolveDeclarationSourceFile`. The map is EMPTY under the embedded lib (single file), so the embedded path is byte-identical — any NEW TS2728 lib site must do the same node-first check or it will misattribute under real libs. The `DEPRECATED_STRING_HTML_HELPERS` override still fires on top (it only needs `isLib` true, which the map now guarantees).
- **`verbatimModuleSyntax` suppresses const enum inlining**: When set, skip `collectConstEnumValues`, skip checker's `resolveConstEnumMemberAccess`, keep const enum IIFE bodies, and don't treat const enums as type-only.

### `this`-typing in value resolution (B101, round 115)

- **Do NOT wire a concrete enclosing-class `this` type into `getTypeOfIdentifier("this")` (the general value-resolution path).** It FPs the RETURN-assignability path: `return this.x.y().then(...)` resolves the receiver chain to an incomplete generic `Promise<T>` (unresolved T), and `checkReturnAssignability` then compares it against a named target → bogus TS2739/TS2322 (regressed `variableDeclaratorResolvedDuringContextualTyping`). For a `this.member`-shape diagnostic, use a NARROW per-shape check gated on a behavior-neutral `currentClassForThis: ClassDeclaration?` field (set per-INSTANCE-member in the assignability walker's ClassDeclaration handler, reset for static members + non-arrow nested-function boundaries that rebind `this`). B101's `tryEmitVoidThisMethodToPrimitiveVar` (`var x: <primitive> = this.voidMethod()` → TS2322) is the reference. Also note: `getTypeOfSymbol`'s MethodDeclaration branch defaults an UN-annotated method's return to `anyType`, diverging from `resolveInterfaceMembers` which infers `void` for a no-return body. **The old "net-zero standalone" claim is STALE — B420 (2026-06-14) measured that broadening the method branch to `bodyHasNoReturn → voidType` now REGRESSES 5 tests (getAndSetNotIdenticalType2/3, inheritanceMemberAccessorOverridingMethod ×2, inheritanceMemberAccessorOverridingProperty): `() => void` vs `() => any` changes accessor/override assignability.** If you need the `void` rendering for a DISPLAY (e.g. an elaboration chain), do it locally from the declaration (B420's `renderSymbolTypeWithVoidInference` renders a no-`return` un-annotated MethodDeclaration body as `(...) => void`), NOT by changing the symbol-type branch.
- **A new full-tree class walker that calls `getDeclaredTypeOfClassOrInterface` / `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` for EVERY class it visits poisons type caches by FIRST-TOUCH ordering** (CLAUDE.md "resolution context at first touch wins"). B420's constructor-return check regressed `getAndSetNotIdenticalType2/3` + `inheritanceMemberAccessorOverriding*` until it was gated to resolve the class type ONLY when a constructor actually returns the shape it checks (an object-literal / new-expression). Collect the work-list AST-first; resolve types lazily only for classes that have work.
- **TS2722 for invoking a possibly-undefined OPTIONAL member (`bar.optionalMethod(1)` where `optionalMethod?` is optional+callable) is a dedicated check at the top of `checkSingleCallExpressionTypes` (round 332)** — `getCalleeType` returns the BARE callable for an optional-member access (we don't add `| undefined` there), so the call would otherwise validate. Gated: `strictNullChecks`, no optional-chaining on the call OR the access (`!expr.questionDotToken && !pa.questionDotToken`), the member resolves OPTIONAL + callable on the receiver type, and the access is NOT flow-narrowed (`propertyAccessNarrowedNonNull` builds `<propType>|undefined` and asks `getNarrowedTypeForReference` whether undefined survives — suppresses a guarded `if(x.m)x.m()`). Span = the whole `recv.m` property-access (matching tsc). Only 12 corpus baselines carry TS2722 → bounded surface.

### TS2683 implicit-`this` gotchas (round 79h)

- **TS2683 fires by DEFAULT unless `@strict: false` is explicit**: `checkImplicitThis` is gated `options.noImplicitThis || options.strict || !options.strictExplicitlyFalse` — same convention as TS2454/TS2564. TypeScript's compiler-test baselines (`thisInModuleFunction1`, `thisInInnerFunctions`, `varArgParamTypeCheck`, `thisInClassBodyStaticESNext`) set NEITHER flag yet expect TS2683. Do NOT narrow the gate back to `noImplicitThis || strict`.
- **Callback args whose callee param type declares `(this: T, …)` are contextually-this-typed → TS2683 suppressed** (`callArgHasContextualThis`); the helper is CONSERVATIVE — any resolution failure returns false so TS2683 still fires (never a false suppression). Var-annotation contextual-`this` (`const x: FnTypeWithThis = function(){…}`) is NOT yet handled.

### TS7009 implicit-any `new` gotchas (round 79i)

- **TS7009 = `new F()` where F lacks a CONSTRUCT signature — fires REGARDLESS of F's return annotation** (a function's `new`-instance type is not its call-return type). Gate is `noImplicitAny || strict`, NOT the `!strictExplicitlyFalse` harness-default TS2683 uses (`checkImplicitAnyNewExpressions`).
- **`new <T>Expr()` (leading type args) is NOT a clean `new Expr()`**: TypeScript parses `new <any>Test2()` as a comparison expression (`new < any > Test2()`) → TS2365/TS1109/TS2693, NOT TS7009. Our parser recovers an Identifier target, so `checkNewExprImplicitAny` must skip when `expr.leadingTypeArguments` is non-empty or it spuriously emits TS7009.

### TS7006 contextual-typing gotchas (round 431)

- **The TS7006 walker has TWO signature-selection rules that must NOT be unified**: `contextualCallableArity` (max arity over ALL sigs — annotation/object-member/return contexts, deliberately permissive) vs `singleApplicableSigArity` (assignment contexts, the `ctxViaAssignment` flag — B476's rule: a ≥2-arity-applicable-sig LHS provides NO context, pinned by contextualTypingWithGenericAndNonGenericSignature's FIRE). Binary ctx propagation: `||`/`??` feed BOTH operands, `&&`/comma the RIGHT only (contextuallyTypeLogicalAnd03/CommaOperator03 pin the left firing); an UNTYPED local LHS (`let mark; mark = tag => …`) must keep firing (uncalledFunctionChecksInConditional2).
- **`implicitAnyScopes` (the walker's lexical scope stack) is maintained by push/pop in try/finally at EVERY function-like boundary** — a new body-walk site in the implicit-any walker must push its params or callee/assignment-target resolution silently falls to file symbols. `isCalleeResolvable` also consults the nested-fn name map (`anyNestedFunctionNamed` — B83.5-unbound nested decls); scope-map values are declared ANNOTATIONS (null = declared untyped → fire), plus `as T` casts and callee return annotations for call-initialized locals. **THREE parallel stacks since round 464** (`implicitAnyScopes` / `implicitAnyScopeInits` / `implicitAnyScopeDestructures` — the last maps a destructured element name → its source expression + property name, so an assignment target rooted at a destructured local resolves its contextual type from the source's declared member); push/pop ONLY via `pushImplicitAnyScope`/`popImplicitAnyScope` or the stacks drift out of index alignment.

### Return-path foreign-TP gate (round 431c/d) — coupled pair, do not touch one side alone

- **`checkReturnAssignability` bails on a source containing a FOREIGN TypeParam** (`typeContainsForeignTypeParam`: TP name ∉ the enclosing fn's `typeParams`; walks unions/intersections/Reference args/tuples + ANONYMOUS-object members/call-sigs — named interfaces only via Reference args): an un-inferred generic call result (`return append(…)` typing as `T[]`) is OUR inference gap, not a user error. **This gate and the round-431c `returnTypeNode` threading through the switch/try/if dispatcher arms are LOAD-BEARING as a PAIR** — un-threading regresses the VisitResult family; removing the gate floods ~118 un-inferred-generic return FPs. Round 431e extended the gate to the var-decl/assignment/property-write/conditional-return paths (property-write passes an EMPTY own-set — no typeParams threading there). **A SIGNATURE's own `typeParameters` count as BOUND within that signature** — a generic FUNCTION-VALUE source (`var f: (x: number) => number = genericFn`) is legitimately checkable; dropping the sig-own rule regresses genericAssignmentCompatOfFunctionSignatures1 + 4 sibling corpus pins.
- **TS2367 same-target-Reference disjointness requires a differing arg pair anchored in a NON-object type** (`Array<string>` vs `Array<number>` fires; `NodeArray<TypeNode>` vs `NodeArray<Node>` bails) — an object-vs-object both-directions relation failure is indistinguishable from an M3 relation gap.

### TS7057 / TS4025-globalThis / TS8021 FP-firewall gates (round 106) — load-bearing, do NOT broaden

- **TS8021 (`checkJSDocTypedefTags`) is JS-only and purely syntactic**: fires ONLY for `@typedef` with no `{type}` AND no `@property`/`@member` anywhere in the same comment block — do not broaden beyond that shape.

### Chained array-method type flow (Blocker #2, round 123, B136)

- **Array predicate-overload selection**: `resolveCallOverload` matches by ARITY and picks the FIRST overload; when the chosen overload's return contains an unresolved TP and the call's arg is NOT a type-guard predicate (`callHasTypeGuardArg`), `getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` prefers a NON-GENERIC arity-matching overload with a concrete return (a genuine `(x): x is Foo` arg keeps the generic overload). The property-access walker also types UNANNOTATED locals into `currentLocalTypes` — deliberately gated to CallExpression initializers with concrete resolved types to bound blast radius; do not broaden to other initializer kinds without a failing-set diff.
- **Broad type-flow changes must be verified by FAILING-SET DIFF** (git-stash baseline run, diff the failing-test sets) — the pass COUNT alone can mask a +N/−M swap.

### TS7022+TS7024 circular generic-callback initializer (round 127, B147)

- **TS7023 indirect-self-reference-return (`checkIndirectSelfReferenceReturn`) must NOT fire for a self-reference that appears ONLY as a CALLBACK ARGUMENT** (`return cond ? mapType(t, self) : concrete;` — tsc's own `getMutableArrayOrTupleType`/`unwrapAwaitedType`): a self passed as a call argument receives a contextual parameter type from the callee's signature, so self's own return type is never needed to type the call, and the other branches supply a concrete type → no cycle. `selfRefsOnlyAsCallbackArgs(expr, name)` is the gate (a self-occurrence is safe iff it's a direct `CallExpression` argument; array/object element, element-access base, property receiver, operand, callee stay stuck → `[self][0]()`, `{next: self}` STILL fire). Do NOT count a callback-arg self-ref toward `anyIndirect`.

### TS2729 use-before-initialization gotchas (round 79j)

- **A declared-ABOVE property fires TS2729 only when `!hasInit && !hasExcl && !isOptional`** (`collectThisPropertyRefs`/`checkClassPropertyUseBeforeInit`): an OPTIONAL `p5?: number` declared above and read in a later field initializer is legitimately `undefined` → NOT use-before-init; a non-optional un-initialized `abstract prop: string` IS. Do NOT blanket declared-above to "never fires" (regresses `abstractPropertyInConstructor`) nor to "always fires when no init" (regresses optional `p5`).
- **A write-target is not a use**: `this.p6 = "x"` in another field's initializer must NOT count as using `p6`. In the `BinaryExpression` branch, for `operator == SyntaxKind.Equals` with a `this.X`/`ClassName.X` LHS, recurse only into the LHS receiver (so `this.a.b = …` still uses `this.a`) + RHS — skip the LHS name ref.
- **TS2729: object-literal accessor/method COMPUTED NAMES in field initializers count only when NOT spread-nested** (`viaSpread` flag in `collectThisPropertyRefs`, matches TypeScript); accessor/method BODIES are always deferred (never recursed); ClassExpression `extends` heritage IS recursed (eager evaluation).

### Contextually-typed-arrow return-mismatch gotcha (round 79l)

- **A function-typed argument's return-type mismatch reports TS2322-at-the-arrow-return (+ related TS6502) for NON-GENERIC callees, but the coarse whole-arg TS2345 for GENERIC-instantiated callees.** `foo(b: (item:number)=>boolean)` called `foo(a => a)` → TS2322 "Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'boolean'." at the arrow's return expr + TS6502 "The expected type comes from the return type of this signature." at the callback FunctionType node's `.pos` (the `(`, NOT the return-type node). But `_.map<T,U,V>(c, (x,y)=>...)` (generic, explicit type args) keeps the whole-arg TS2345. The conversion in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (inside the `!isSimpleCheckableType(paramType)` block, gated by `allowFuncReturnMismatch`) is double-gated: `!calleeGenericInstantiation` (a param set `true` at the explicit-type-args instantiation call site ~68686) AND `sigIn.typeParameters.isNullOrEmpty()` (catches un-instantiated generic sigs). Both gates are load-bearing — dropping either regresses `genericCombinators2` / `contextualTypingOfGenericFunctionTypedArguments1`.

### TS2528 multiple default exports gotchas

- **Anonymous FD/CD position**: Error at `export` keyword, NOT `function`/`class` — `FunctionDeclaration.pos` = `function` keyword, must search backwards for `export`.
- **2752/2753/6204 code exception**: When the LAST export is a `FunctionDeclaration`, codes swap: non-lasts use TS2752, last uses TS2753.
- **TS2323 vs TS2528 can fire simultaneously**: `emitTs2323 = declCount >= 2`; `emitTs2528 = hasNonDeclInline || !emitTs2323`.
- **`ExportSpecifier.name.pos` includes trivia**: Skip whitespace to get token start position for diagnostics.

### Top-level await gotchas

- **Parser `topLevelAwait` parameter**: When `module` is ES2022+, NodeNext, Preserve, or System, the Parser's `topLevelAwait` flag must be set to `true`. This sets initial `inAsyncContext = true` at the file level, enabling `await` keyword recognition at the top level. Inside function bodies, `inAsyncContext` is still reset per-function based on `async` modifier. In sync functions, `await(x)` remains a call expression (identifier).

### Const enum type-only treatment gotchas

- **Erased const enums must be type-only in ALL pre-scans**: `earlyPureTypeNames`, `pureTypeNames`, `directExportedVarNames`, and `topLevelTypeOnlyNames` — both locally-declared and imported. Without this, `export = ConstEnum` generates wrong output.
- **Binder `export { X }` must not overwrite value symbols**: For local re-exports without `from` clause, skip creating an Alias if the name is already declared as a value.
- **`.d.ts` module resolution only for relative specifiers**: Non-relative specifiers might find `.d.ts` files with augmented ambient modules, making TS2694 checks unreliable.
- **Ambient module `.d.ts` exports use inner module exports**: When resolving to a `.d.ts` with `declare module "X" {}`, use that module's exports, NOT file-level locals.
- **Default import resolution chain**: Resolve `import Foo from "./mod"` in order: (1) `locals["default"]`, (2) `ExportAssignment` (not `isExportEquals`), (3) `export { X as default } from`.

### Misc engine gotchas (lib coupling / display / caches — historically misfiled under "Decorator metadata")

- **Same-named `export interface` in different files MERGES via `mergeSymbolTable`**: At checker init, `mergeSymbolTable(globals, result.locals)` is called for every binderResult. When two files both `export interface F`, the second file's F is merged into the first's symbol — `existing.declarations.addAll(symbol.declarations)`. Downstream, both files' `let a: A.F` and `let b: B.F` resolve through the SAME merged symbol, and after `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` both Type.Interface instances carry the UNION of properties from all declarations. Consequence: structural disjointness checks (`b53.2`-style "no overlap" TS2367) silently never fire because the "missing" property has been merged into both sides. This is part of **Blocker #3 (Cross-file global scope conflation)** — fixing requires per-file scope construction with only own-file declarations in own-file resolution. Workaround patterns: diagnostics that compare ENTIRE display strings (TS2741 in B53.1) work because separate Type.Interface IDs survive merging; diagnostics that compare member-sets do not.
- **`LIB_MIN_TARGET` member filtering applies in `resolveInterfaceMembers`'s per-decl loop** (gated `decl in builtinLibDecls` + `options.target < minTarget`); user augmentations of the same interface pass through UNFILTERED. Verify new entries against `typescript-repo/src/lib/lib.esXXXX.*.d.ts`. Symptom of a missing entry: a SWAP diff that is JUST a count change in an "and N more" missing-property display. (See also the round-135 Lib-suggestion section for SOFT entries and `libFeatureAvailable`.)
- **The embedded lib deliberately simplifies most Array callback methods to `=> any`/`any[]`** — only `map`/`reduce`/`reduceRight` are generic (`ReadonlyArray.map` too since round 466, mirroring `Array.map`) and `every`/`filter`/`find`/`findLast` carry the real type-predicate overload pairs (see BUILTIN_LIB_SOURCE). Promoting ANOTHER Array method to generic is a BROAD lib change touching every array-method call — full-suite verify. **Rounds 455/456: `ReadonlyArray.filter`/`.every`/`.find` + `Array.find`/`.findLast` now carry the SAME type-predicate overload (`find<S extends T>(predicate: … => value is S, thisArg?): S | undefined`) — `arr.find(isFoo)` refines to `S | undefined`, not the base `T | undefined` (`tryInferPredicateOverloadReturn` infers S; utilities.ts `symbol.declarations?.find(isClassLike)`). Adding an OVERLOAD to an EXISTING method (not a new member name) does NOT shift "and N more" missing-property counts. Mirror any NEW Array type-guard overload to ReadonlyArray, and vice versa.**
- **Destructured `[x]`/`{x}` params are NOT typed in `currentLocalTypes`** (`populateParameterLocalTypes` registers only annotated + function-default Identifier params there; binding names go to `currentParamBindingNames`), **but since round 429 `getTypeOfIdentifier` consults the side set (AFTER `currentLocalTypes`) and returns anyType** — a destructured param no longer falls through to a same-named merged-global function/var in bare-identifier positions. A concrete `currentLocalTypes` registration still wins; shadow-detection diagnostics still consult the set directly. **Round 453: an UN-ANNOTATED non-function-default Identifier param is ALSO added to `currentParamBindingNames` (→ anyType) AND overrides an INHERITED same-named `currentLocalTypes` entry with anyType** — a param must shadow a same-named top-level module-level var that leaked into `globals`/`currentLocalTypes` (program.ts's `const indent = "    "` shadowed by a param `indent = 0`; the recursive arg resolved to the leaked const's `string` → FP TS2345). Un-annotated-only (annotated params keep their type). The `varTypes` (string) side of this leak-shadow is NOT fixed (the assignment/return walk's `innerTypes` override didn't reach the return-check — reverted; not a confirmed dashboard FP).
- **`<ident> = undefined` against an OPTIONAL/`T|undefined` target is legal — the identifier-target assignment check (Checker.kt ~88831) bails when the RHS is literally `undefined`, `!exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, and the DECLARED engine type (`narrowedDeclaredTypes[name] ?: currentLocalTypes[name]`) includes undefined (round 453).** The string `varTypes` drops an optional param's `?` (`resolveSimpleTypeName` → "T"), so the string relation FP-fired TS2322 — but the type-engine map carries the correct `T | undefined` (B85.1a for optional params). The identifier-target analog of round 448's `this.optionalProp = undefined`; a non-optional `x: T` target keeps firing.
- **The Binder binds only ONE of a get/set accessor pair into the property symbol's `declarations`**; `resolveInterfaceMembers` appends both (B54.6) — `propSym.declarations.firstOrNull { it is SetAccessor }` is reliable only AFTER member resolution, not on raw binder output.
- **`x.x = v` write-context uses SETTER's param type, NOT getter's return type**: For accessor-pair properties, TypeScript's write-context type check uses the SetAccessor's parameter type (not the property's "main" type). Implemented by B54.5 (2026-05-19) in `checkPropertyAccessAssignment`: before resolving the property's type, look for a SetAccessor declaration on propSym and prefer its parameter type. Pairs with B54.6's accessor-pair declaration merging.
- **`getReturnTypeOfNewExpression` instantiates `C<unknown,…>` ONLY for generic-class `new C()` with NO type args AND NO ctor args** (declared TP defaults win; strictNullChecks-gated). Broadening the gate was tried twice and regressed both times (args-present, no-ctor-decl) — un-instantiated calleeType is structurally compatible with targets `C<unknown>` isn't. The conservative gate is load-bearing.
- **A CLASS-instance callee is EXCLUDED from `getReturnTypeOfNewExpression`'s constructor-interface branch (16.4gi, round 475)** — class instance types DO carry `constructSignatures` (own ctor + INHERITED-FIRST base ctors, per resolveInterfaceMembers), so an ungated `sigs[0].resolvedReturnType` typed `new Subclass(...)` as the BASE class. The branch's old "class instances have no construct sigs" comment was stale; only non-class callees (`declare var X: XConstructor`) take it.
- **`tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` has a full-mapper-or-null CONTRACT — do NOT make it return partial mappers** (`getReturnTypeOfCallExpression` depends on it for return-type substitution). Partial anchor-only inference (bare-`T` / rest-`T[]` / `Array<T>` positions) lives in the SEPARATE consumer fallback `tryInferAnchorTypeParamsForContext`; keep the two concerns in separate helpers.
- **TS2589 fires via the `deepInstantiationBailed` flag** (set on alias-depth bail in `getTypeFromTypeReference`, consumed save/reset/check-style in `buildFileLocalTypeMaps`). The substitution is SKIPPED when any type arg fails its TypeParam constraint (B57.1b) — prevents FP TS2589 where TS2344 owns the error. Covers variable-annotation/alias-body positions only (not params/returns/expressions/mapped-type recursion).
- **`typeParamInternCache` keys by `internKey(tp)` = `(TypeParameter.internSalt, pos)` packed into a Long, NOT bare `pos` (M1.13).** The bare AST `pos` COLLIDES across files in a multi-file program (each file's positions start at 0), so two unrelated params in different files shared ONE `Type.TypeParam` and stomped its mutable `.constraint`/`.default` (a spurious TS2344 in tsc's own sources). `internSalt = fileName.hashCode()` is stamped by the parser (`parseTypeParameter`) onto every TypeParameter; a single-file compile stamps them all with the SAME salt so the key is a bijection with `pos` (corpus byte-identical). A NEW intern site MUST use `internKey(tp)`, never `tp.pos` — and any OTHER pos-keyed cache/map that stores per-declaration mutable state across files has the same latent hazard (grep `getOrPut(...pos)`). Interning is still NOT applied at every TypeParam creation site (some mint fresh instances per resolution, defeating Type-id caches); extending it needs per-site verification (interface call-sig TPs must stay distinct per sig).
- **TS2563 is a faithful flow-walk DEPTH trip (round 426, replacing the B399 per-file node-count proxy)**: a flow walker frame hitting the 2000-recursion limit sets `flowDepthTripped`; every depth-0 walk entry routes through `flowWalkWithTripCheck(reference)`, which reports TS2563 once for the containing function-or-module block (via the flow graph's `containerStarts`) and adds the container's range to `flowDisabledRanges` — subsequent walks in that range bail without walking, and the end-of-init filter removes every TS2454 in a disabled range (tsc's OR-rule per container). THREE invariants: (1) a NEW depth-0 caller of `narrowTypeFromFlow`/`narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry`/`isAssignedAtFlow` MUST wrap in `flowWalkWithTripCheck` — an unwrapped entry leaves a stale trip flag that the next wrapped walk mis-attributes; (2) only the PER-WALK depth limit trips — the visit budget / global re-entry budget / cycle bail stay SILENT truncation (they are our services-perf firewalls, not tsc semantics); (3) linear pass-through antecedents (incl. `isAssignedAtFlow`'s, rewritten iterative round 426) consume NO depth, so a straight-line chain of any length never trips — only branch-join/condition/loop-entry recursion does (tsc `getTypeAtFlowNode`'s while-loop). The TS2454 flow refinement walk runs even for positions the AST-based emitter already reported (trip detection needs the walk; the range filter retracts the earlier emission).
- **errorType displays as 'any' in typeToString (B58.1)**: `Type.Intrinsic("error")` (the errorType singleton) renders as 'any' in diagnostic messages — matches TypeScript's display convention. Other Type.Intrinsic instances (anyType, stringType, etc.) still render via `intrinsicName`. Affects any diagnostic that displays types containing errorType after substitution failure (e.g. recursive aliases that hit depth bail mid-substitution).
- **TS2615's outermost-mapped-type capture gate in `getTypeFromMappedType` includes `!savedBailAtEntry` — load-bearing**: without it, an outermost mapped type whose body completes cleanly but is reached AFTER a sibling depth-bail mis-attributes TS2615 to itself. Consumers save/restore `mappedTypeCircularInfo` alongside `deepInstantiationBailed`.
- **`aliasDisplayMap` misses recursive-alias OUTER types**: fresh Type/TypeParam ids are minted per re-resolution, so `substitutionResultCache` (B58.3) misses and structural-id lookup (B58.4) was tried and FAILED (element ids differ recursively). The remaining fix directions (intern TypeParams at the function-signature site / structural equality with cycle-break / per-call display map) all carry broad regression risk — not a quick fix.

### TS2454/TS2564 gotchas

- **`isAssignedAtFlow` follows `FlowStart.outerFlow` (round 460)** — a CAPTURED read in an expression-bodied arrow assumes the ENCLOSING function's assignments (tsc isOuterVariable); the `localNames` gate keeps a closure-local redeclaration inside. OR-semantics: the continuation only SUPPRESSES; a never-assigned captured read still fires.
- **A destructuring ASSIGNMENT's `FlowAssignment.node` is the WHOLE `({a,b} = X)` BinaryExpression since round 460** (was the leaf Identifier; a bare pattern literal for `for ([a,b] of …)` targets) — `flowAssignmentTargetsName` walks pattern LHSes via `destructuringAssignTargetHasName`, which MUST handle nested default-value patterns (`{ skills: { primary: p = "x" } = {} }`, a corpus pin); `narrowByAssignmentRhs` strips nullish from the DECLARED type when the destructured member type resolves nullish-free (unresolvable keeps the antecedent).
- **TWO disjoint used-before-assigned passes (round 450): the SET-based `checkUsesOfUninitialized`/`markAssignments` (statement-sequenced, does NOT recurse into if/loop BODIES) and the flow-graph `walkStmtForFlowTS2454`/`isAssignedAtFlow` (recurses into bodies, consults the flow graph). They fire in DIFFERENT contexts, so a TS2454 FP may need fixing in one, both, or neither — check which emits before editing.** `markAssignments`' `while(true)` case removes a var via `whileTrueDefinitelyAssigns` — a SOUND single-variable definite-assignment walk (sequential flow + if/else join + abrupt break/return/throw/continue) that BAILS (keeps the var uninitialized, never over-clears) on any labeled break/continue or a `try`/labeled statement that could hide an exiting break; nested loops/switch are opaque. Do NOT "simplify" it to credit inner-loop assignments or drop the bail — that reintroduces unsoundness (missed genuine TS2454). The flow pass drops a `for (const/let X of/in …)` loop var from its uninitialized set when descending into the body (X shadows + is bound each iteration) — same rationale as the catch-body shadowing skip.
- **`any` type skips checking**: Variables/properties typed as `any` skip TS2454/TS2564.
- **`var` declarations**: TypeScript DOES check `var` for TS2454 (when strict) — only `any`-typed vars are skipped.
- **`ExportAssignment` is a use site**: `export = Foo` must be checked in `checkUsesOfUninitialized`.
- **Abstract classes still need TS2564**: Only `declare` classes are exempt, NOT `abstract`.
- **TS7030 guard**: Requires `strictNullChecks || noImplicitReturns`. TS2355 fires regardless.
- **TS2366 requires strictNullChecks**: TS2366 ("Function lacks ending return statement and return type does not include 'undefined'") fires ONLY under `strictNullChecks`. Without it, use TS7030 ("Not all code paths return a value"). Do NOT gate TS2366 on `noImplicitReturns` alone.
- **TS2355 for `undefined` in unions**: TS2355 fires for `function f(): undefined | T {}` (where T is non-void) when there are no return statements — `undefined` in a UNION does NOT satisfy "must return a value", only as a BARE `undefined` keyword (or `Promise<undefined>` for async) does. The classification in `checkBodyForImplicitReturn` (Checker.kt): `KeywordTypeNode(undefined)` and `TypeReference(Promise<KeywordTypeNode(undefined)>)` for async → "pure-undefined" (suppress all checks); UnionType-containing-undefined or `Promise<UnionType>` → "nullable" (TS2355 fires when no returns; TS7030 falls through when mixed returns).
- **`NumericLiteralNode` in method names**: All method-name extraction functions must handle it, not just `Identifier` and `StringLiteralNode`.
- **Bare specifier TS2882**: Always emit for bare (non-relative) specifiers except in AMD/System/UMD modules.

### TS1210 class strict mode gotchas

- **TS1210 vs TS1100**: TS1210 fires for `arguments`/`eval` in class bodies (always strict). TS1100 fires in external strict mode contexts (functions, modules). TypeScript NEVER emits both for the same node — TS1210 takes priority in class bodies. Suppress TS1100 inside class bodies for these names.
- **A property/method NAME is NEVER strict-mode-restricted (TS1100/TS1213/TS1214) — only BINDING names are** (tsc's `checkStrictModeEvalOrArguments`/`checkStrictModeIdentifier` fire for variable/parameter/function names + assignment LHS, gated `!isIdentifierName(node)`). So `interface I { arguments: T; eval(): void }` / `class C { let = 1 }` are legal. `checkStrictModeInStatement`'s `InterfaceDeclaration` branch checks method/index PARAM names only — do NOT re-add a `PropertyDeclaration`/method-name check (round 406 removed one that FP'd TS1100 on tsc's own `interface CallExpression { readonly arguments: NodeArray<Expression>; }`).
- **`emitDeclarationOnly` still needs checker**: Multi-file compilation with `emitDeclarationOnly` must still parse/bind/check files for diagnostics like TS1210. But use `declarationOnly = true` to limit checks — running the full checker produces FPs like TS6131 that TypeScript's declaration-only mode suppresses.
- **`isAlwaysTruthyExpr` vs `isAlwaysTruthyForOrExpr`**: In `||` contexts, TypeScript flags object-like expressions (function, arrow, object, array, class, regex) and non-empty string literals. Numeric literals and `new` expressions are NOT flagged in `||` but ARE flagged in `if`/`else if` conditions.

### Kotlin idioms

- **NEVER key a HashMap/HashSet by an AST NODE with a large subtree (FunctionDeclaration, class/namespace bodies) — AST nodes are Kotlin data classes, so `hashCode()`/`equals()` DEEP-recurse the whole subtree on every lookup** (round 471: a `HashMap<FunctionDeclaration, String>` in buildNestedFunctionMap cost the services self-compile +10 s, 39 → 51 s; replaced by cluster-order-aligned file lists). The existing `builtinLibDecls`/`builtinLibMemberDecls` node-sets survive because lib decl subtrees are small — not a licence. Key by `Symbol.id`/`Type.id`/pos-based keys, or keep parallel lists aligned to an existing order.

- **Kotlin's `mutableMapOf()`/`mutableSetOf()` return LinkedHashMap/LinkedHashSet — ordered, so they pay `LinkedHashMap.afterNodeInsertion` on every put and an ordered copy on construction (round 483: ~5% of the compiler self-compile self-time was `afterNodeInsertion`).** On a HOT, order-INDEPENDENT map/set — especially one COPIED per scope entry — write `HashMap()`/`HashSet()` (and `HashMap(other)`/`HashSet(other)` for copies, NOT `.toMutableMap()`/`.toMutableSet()`, which always return the linked variant). The per-function-body scope structures (`currentLocalTypes`, `currentLocalDeclTypeNodes`, `currentShadowedNames`, `currentParamBindingNames`) and `getUnionType`'s dedup set are HashMap/HashSet for exactly this reason. **Before converting, PROVE order is unused**: grep the field for `.keys/.values/.entries/.forEach/.map/.iterator/.first/.joinToString/.sorted` — a map whose iteration feeds DISPLAY or DIAGNOSTIC output (member order, union display) MUST stay ordered, so this is a per-field audit, not a blanket sweep.
- **The build is WARNING-CLEAN (0 compiler warnings; the 2026-07-04 sweep cleared 1,025) — KEEP IT THAT WAY: fix every new compiler `w:` the moment it appears, never let them accumulate.** `extraWarnings = true` + `progressiveMode = true` (build.gradle.kts) mean some warnings become HARD ERRORS on a Kotlin bump (any "will become an error in language version 2.4" note), and a warning routinely flags a REAL BUG — the sweep found `minArgumentCount` was always 0 because `it.questionToken == null` compares a `Boolean` (never null). Check with `./gradlew compileKotlinJvm compileTestKotlinJvm --rerun-tasks 2>&1 | grep -a '^w:'` (needs `--rerun-tasks`; Gradle caches by content hash and does NOT re-emit warnings on an up-to-date compile). Every fix must be behavior-preserving — gate on the full corpus suite; `USELESS_CAST`/redundant-`!!`/redundant-`?.` are identical static types (safe), but "always true/false" and unused-var removals can drop a load-bearing smart-cast or side effect (a bare `x as T` statement smart-casts `x`), so compile + suite after each batch.
- **`*/` inside KDoc terminates the comment**: A `/** ... */` doc comment ends at the first `*/`. Embedding `*/` (e.g. when documenting a JSDoc pattern) silently breaks the rest of the declaration — a data-class body following a broken doc comment surfaces as a cascade of confusing errors like "Modifier 'override' is not applicable to 'local variable'", "No parameter with name 'pos' found", "Unresolved reference 'jsDocTagEnd'". Reword to "comment-close" or escape as `*\/`.
- **Kotlin block comments NEST — a literal `/**` *inside* a KDoc opens a nested comment level (B151)**: writing the JSDoc string `/** @overload ... */` inside a `/** ... */` doc comment makes the inner `/**` open a NESTED block comment, so the doc comment's closing `*/` only drops back to depth 1 and the comment runs to EOF → `Syntax error: Unclosed comment` at the last line + a cascade of "Unresolved reference" errors for class members BEFORE the doc comment (the whole class fails to parse). This is DISTINCT from the `*/`-closes-early gotcha above: here it's the `/**` opener that bites. When documenting JSDoc tag syntax in a KDoc, never write a literal `/**` (or `/*`) — describe it as "a JSDoc comment" or use single-line `//` comments. **WORSE, SILENT variant (round 390, build.gradle.kts): a path GLOB in a KDoc (e.g. writing src-slash-lib-slash-star-dot-d-dot-ts) also contains the nested-comment opener — and when a LATER declaration's `*/` happens to re-balance the depth, the file COMPILES with a silently-DEAD region: tasks/vals declared inside it simply don't exist ("Task not found", top-level statements never run), and even a syntax error appended inside the swallowed span "builds successfully". If a build-script region mysteriously does not execute, scan comment-nesting depth before anything else.**
- **No non-stdlib dependencies in `commonMain`**: The project targets Kotlin Native (in addition to JVM/JS), so `commonMain` must use only `kotlin.*` and `kotlinx.*` packages. No `java.*`, no `BigDecimal`, no JVM-only types. Use Kotlin's built-in numeric types and stdlib math (`kotlin.math.*`).
- **Enum context resolution** (Kotlin 2.1+): When the expected type is an enum, use unqualified entry names — write `Equals`, not `SyntaxKind.Equals`. This applies to `when` branch conditions, named arguments (`operator = Equals`), comparisons (`flags == VarKeyword`), and any other position where the enum type is inferred. Caveat: if a data class has the same name as an enum entry (e.g. `LabeledStatement`), keep the `SyntaxKind.` prefix to avoid ambiguity.
- **`in 0..<x` range checks**: Prefer `pos in 0..<end` over `pos >= 0 && end > pos` for range validation — uses Kotlin's `rangeUntil` (`..<`) operator for exclusive upper bound.
- **No JVM-only APIs in `commonMain`**: `Map.putIfAbsent` → use `getOrPut`; `Math.pow` → use `kotlin.math.pow` extension; `RegexOption.DOT_MATCHES_ALL` → use `[\s\S]` in the pattern (B259). `./gradlew compileKotlinJvm` does NOT catch these — it compiles only the JVM target. Always use Kotlin stdlib equivalents for multiplatform compatibility.
- **`when` guard conditions** (Kotlin 2.1+): Use `when (val ch = x) { '/' if condition -> ... }` instead of `when { ch == '/' && condition -> ... }`. The `if` guard after the match value keeps pattern matching readable and avoids nested `when`/`if` blocks.

## AI agent mission

**Phase 17: Self-compile the TypeScript compiler, then performance** (owner directive 2026-07-03, re-scoping 2026-07-02's "any TypeScript project": fully compile the TypeScript compiler ITSELF, then optimize performance — the v1 exit is all 8 tsc-source profiles at zero FPs, all files emitted, zero crashes; "any project" and the items that only serve it (M2.4/M3.0/M3.5/M4) are parked in `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` § "Post-v1 backlog", which the loop skips until v1 lands). Pipeline: Scanner → Parser → **Binder → Checker → Transformer → Emitter**. The corpus suite (Phase 16, 100% green) is now a permanent zero-regression GATE, not the goal; the success metric is the real-world dashboard (per-project FP counts / emit diffs / crashes / throughput — see `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` § "The dashboard" and `bench/*.tsv`). Live counts in `STATUS.md`. The M3 engine items' accumulated analysis lives in `PLAN-PHASE-4.md` § "Known architectural blockers" — read it before starting one. **M5 was re-scoped 2026-07-13 (owner) to the staged architecture-inversion arc (INV.0–INV.7): read `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` before ANY M5/INV work — flat-profile micro-opt rounds are closed; kotlinx-coroutines-core in commonMain is owner-approved for the Flow front-end / parallel phases.**

### Execution protocol

PLAN-PHASE-5.md contains the **live QUEUE** under the heading `## Phase 17 — Self-compile the TypeScript compiler (M0–M5)` → `### QUEUE — work top-to-bottom; promote unblockers per protocol` (its `### Post-v1 backlog` section is parked — skip it until v1 lands). (PLAN-PHASE-4.md and its earlier `### QUEUE` sections are archived from prior phases — do not work them.) Live session notes also live under that heading, with the most recent at the top. Default order is top-to-bottom, **fixing as many items per session as the budget allows** — do not stop after a single item if there is remaining context and more tractable work ahead. The outer loop:

1. Pick the first unchecked (`- [ ]`) item in the live queue (or next unfinished sub-step of an `IN PROGRESS` item). If the top item appears blocked on missing infrastructure, **do NOT skip it** — instead, promote the unblocker to the top of the queue and work that (see autonomous-decision policy below). The original item stays unchecked and gets picked up automatically once the unblocker lands. Skipping past a blocker is reserved for the narrow user-policy cases listed under "Guardrails."
2. Implement it — the item describes the deliverable. **Every fix ALSO gets hand-written LOCAL tests for its corner cases** (a `*Test.kt` in `src/commonTest/kotlin/`, kotlin.test — user directive 2026-07-02): the generated corpus suite only pins baseline bytes for corpus shapes; the local test pins the fix's INVARIANT beyond them (both associativity directions, nested contexts, off-corpus depths/inputs). Assert the SHARP signal, not just success — e.g. `diagnostics.none { it.code == 2589 }`, because a boundary-guard-masked stack overflow still "passes" (`DeepExpressionChainTest` is the reference; its first run caught 3 latent overflows the corpus depth missed).
3. Run the full suite (`rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest/binary && ./gradlew jvmTest 2>&1 | grep -a "tests completed"`).
4. Verify no regressions from the currently passing test count.
5. Check off the item (`- [x]`), **bump STATUS.md** (corpus count if it moved, dashboard metrics if the item moved them), add a session note to PLAN-PHASE-5.md (under the live notes heading, most recent on top), add a CLAUDE.md gotcha if applicable, **commit and push** (one commit per sub-step — keeps history bisectable and lets the next agent pick up mid-stream without re-running everything). STATUS.md drift is a real failure mode — past sessions committed `(+N)` work without bumping the count, so the next agent saw a stale baseline. **Trim-on-write (the anti-balloon rule)**: when bumping STATUS.md, keep only the ~5 most-recent round notes — move older ones to docs/history/STATUS-HISTORY.md in the same commit; when adding a session note to PLAN-PHASE-5.md, if more than ~10 rounds are live, move the oldest to docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-5-HISTORY.md in the same commit. Both files ballooned (160 KB / 1.7 MB) before the 2026-06-10 trim because sessions appended without trimming.
6. **Loop back to step 1** and pick up the next item. Keep going until one of these stop conditions:
   - Context budget is running out — finish the current item cleanly, commit, then stop.
   - Every remaining queue item is gated on user-policy decisions (Guardrails) AND no in-scope unblocker work remains. This should be rare — under the promote-unblocker default, blockers turn into queue items, so the queue rarely runs dry.
7. End the session with a concise summary of items completed, unblockers promoted (with what they unblock), items left pending user decisions, and net test count delta.

**Autonomous-decision policy (when an item appears blocked):**

When an item can't be implemented surgically — e.g. the diagnostic relies on infrastructure that doesn't exist yet (super-keyword resolution, full flow-graph, generic argument inference) — **promote the unblocker, do not skip the item**:

- **Promote-unblocker (default)**: identify the smallest concrete infrastructure piece that would unblock the item, insert it at the TOP of the queue with a one-line description and a back-pointer to the original item (and any other items it would also unblock — list them). Then start work on the unblocker. The original item stays unchecked; it gets picked up automatically on a future loop. Cross-phase work (binder + checker together, scope-chain restructuring, new cache layers) is in scope — that is exactly the work this policy exists to drive.
- **Decompose first if large**: if the unblocker is itself a multi-step change, split it into the smallest standalone sub-step that lands as a single commit without regressions, queue that, and only that. Do not attempt the whole infrastructure piece in one pass. Subsequent sub-steps get queued as separate items as the work progresses.
- **Time budget per attempt**: cap each attempt at one full-suite verification cycle. If it regresses, revert. After revert, you may either (a) try a different decomposition for the same unblocker, or (b) move to the next queue item — but record both attempts in the session note. Do not iterate more than twice in one session on the same regressing approach; if neither works, write up what you learned (which paths broke, what the underlying coupling is) and queue a follow-up "investigate X" item.
- **Skip is reserved for Guardrails only**: the only legitimate skip is when the unblocker requires a user-level decision listed under Guardrails below. For those, leave a `BLOCKED-PENDING-USER:` marker on the item with a one-paragraph proposal for the user to react to, then continue with the next queue item. Do NOT end the session.
- **No silent abandonment**: every promote/decompose/skip writes a session note (PLAN-PHASE-4.md, most recent on top) with root cause, what was attempted, and what landed. Never leave an item half-done in the working tree.

**Anti-loop rule — recon-only sessions are a protocol failure:**

(Phase-17 note: the find_candidates machinery below is CORPUS-era — in Phase 17 the live pool is simply the PLAN-PHASE-5.md QUEUE plus any dashboard regression, so "the pool is dry" is never true while unchecked queue items remain. find_candidates stays useful for diagnosing corpus regressions. The no-recon-only-sessions principle applies unchanged.)

A "recon-only session" is one that runs `find_candidates.py --fresh`, gets `0/0/0` (or similarly empty), spot-checks a handful of candidates, confirms they are all architectural, commits a `chore(status): refresh ... empty pool` note, and ends without landing any code change. **This is not a legitimate session outcome when the queue contains unchecked Blocker items.**

- **The `0/0/0` "exhausted" reading is almost always FALSE (fixed 2026-05-30).** The plain `find_candidates.py` EXTRA/MISSING/SWAP buckets only scan tests that ALREADY emit a parseable `error TSxxxx` line — historically ~33 of >1000 failures. The other ~70% (tests where we emit NOTHING, plus pure JS/decl/sourcemap output diffs) were structurally invisible, which is what manufactured the recurring "pool exhausted" illusion across ~20 rounds. The tool now has a **`--none`** bucket (baseline expects diagnostics, we emit none — recovered by reading the `*.errors.txt` baseline, grouped by code, fewest-error-lines-first = most tractable) and an **`--output`** bucket. **The bounded NONE tail IS the live surgical pool** (TS2307 module-resolution, TS2403, TS2367, TS2540, TS2554, TS2688, TS2344, TS2305, TS2551/2552, decl-emit TS4023/4025 …). See PLAN-PHASE-4.md § "STRATEGIC MAP of the remaining failures" for the current breakdown.
- **Hard rule**: the pool is "exhausted" ONLY when `find_candidates.py --fresh` (EXTRA/MISSING/SWAP) **and** `find_candidates.py --none --fresh` (the bounded-code NONE tail) **and** `find_candidates.py --output --fresh` are ALL dry. If any has fresh bounded candidates, work them — do NOT jump to an architectural blocker or commit a recon note. Only when all three are genuinely dry AND the queue has an unchecked `- [ ]` Blocker substep do you start that substep in the same session.
- **Diagnostic signal**: before starting a session, run `git log --oneline -10`. If three or more of the last 10 commits match `chore(status): refresh ... empty pool` (or equivalent recon-only commits), the queue is mis-structured — the agent in earlier sessions failed to promote a blocker to a checkbox. **Your first action is to fix the queue**: open PLAN-PHASE-4.md's "Known architectural blockers" section, pick the highest-yield blocker that has no corresponding `- [ ]` queue item, decompose it into the smallest standalone substep, insert it at the top of the QUEUE, commit that restructure (`chore(queue): promote Blocker #N to queue item`), and only THEN start work on it.
- **Recon is allowed as part of step 1**, not as a session deliverable. If a recon turns up surgical candidates, fine — work them. If it doesn't, that's the trigger to attack the next Blocker substep, not to wrap up.
- **Forbidden commit message patterns** when the queue has unchecked Blocker items: `chore(status): refresh post-N.N recon`, `... empty surgical pool`, `... N+ consecutive empty pool`. These are signals that the protocol has failed. The only acceptable session-end commits are (a) a feature commit landing real code, (b) a `chore(queue): promote ...` restructure that prepares the next session, (c) a `chore(maint)`-class action like a stale-skip-log audit when one is genuinely warranted (≤ once per ~5 sessions), or (d) a revert of a regressing attempt.
- **STATUS.md "consecutive empty pool" counters are a code smell, not a metric**: bumping `19+ → 20+ → 21+ → 22+` across consecutive sessions means each of those sessions failed the anti-loop rule. If you find such a counter on entry, treat it as the diagnostic signal above and fix the queue first.

**Guardrails requiring user input** (do NOT decide autonomously):
- Build system changes (Gradle config, dependency add/remove/upgrade, Kotlin version bump).
- Test-generation pipeline changes (`generateTypeScriptTests`, baseline format, skipped-tests log structure).
- Re-enabling currently-disabled test categories (e.g. `.errors.txt` → +9k tests).
- EXCEPTIONS pre-approved 2026-07-02 (see PLAN-PHASE-5.md § Approvals): conformance-category adoption in the generator (M3.0), the real-lib migration (M2), and the M5 native-target re-enable.

**Other rules:**
- **Do NOT switch items** mid-task — finish the current item (commit + push) before starting the next.
- **Commit between items** — never bundle two unrelated sub-steps into one commit. Each committed sub-step leaves the repo in a clean state for the next agent.
- **Analysis items** (item 0) should produce written artifacts (design docs, categorized lists) before any code is written.
- **No regressions** — the currently passing tests must continue to pass after every change. Re-run the full suite between sub-steps; a +1 / -2 swap is still a regression.
- **Full-suite run caveat**: a clean JVM test run takes ~4-6 minutes. Budget accordingly when deciding whether to attempt another item.

### Reference TypeScript sources

The original TypeScript compiler source is in `typescript-repo/src/compiler/` (if cloned — only test fixtures are present in the sparse clone). When a fix is ambiguous or behavior is unclear, consult TypeScript's public documentation and source on GitHub. The 1M context window can accommodate large files when needed.

## AI agent workflow

- Keep this file and `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` up to date after each session so the next agent/developer starts with accurate state.
- `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` is the live plan (Phase 17 queue + dashboard + session notes). `PLAN-PHASE-4.md` is archived Phase-16 state — its "Known architectural blockers" section is still the reference for the M3 engine items; `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4-HISTORY.md` has everything archived — older session notes, completed queue items, the explored-but-skipped SKIP LOG (add new skip entries there, above the `### End of skip log` terminator), the strategic-map archive, gotchas moved out of CLAUDE.md, and the full Phase 5–15 record. `STATUS.md` keeps the headline test count plus the last ~5 round notes; older entries live in `docs/history/STATUS-HISTORY.md`. `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-3.md` has deferred Phase 3 items (pre-phase-17 artifacts — DESIGN docs, PLAN.md, NEXT-SESSION.md, TYPESCRIPT-TEST-HARNESS.md — also live in `docs/history/`). When grepping for whether a test was previously triaged, grep BOTH `PLAN-PHASE-4.md` and `docs/history/PLAN-PHASE-4-HISTORY.md`.
- For subagent-based parallel work (rarely needed — most surgical fixes are faster done in-line), see `docs/subagent-workflow.md`.

## How to run tests

```bash
# Full suite (always clean binary cache first — stale cache inflates failure count):
rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest/binary && ./gradlew jvmTest 2>&1 | grep -a "tests completed"

# Single test (get expected vs actual diff):
rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest/binary && ./gradlew jvmTest --tests '*.<TestName>*' 2>&1 | grep -a -A 40 "message" | head -50

# Candidate finder (must have fresh full-suite XMLs first):
python3 scripts/find_candidates.py --fresh              # EXTRA/MISSING/SWAP, hide skip-logged
python3 scripts/find_candidates.py                      # all buckets, with [SKIP] markers
python3 scripts/find_candidates.py --none --fresh       # tests where we emit NOTHING (the real pool)
python3 scripts/find_candidates.py --none --fresh --code TS2307   # focus one diagnostic code
python3 scripts/find_candidates.py --output --fresh     # JS/decl/sourcemap output diffs
python3 scripts/find_candidates.py --none --fresh --tsgo # hide tsgo-IRRELEVANT failures (see below)
```
**Skip-log filtering caveat (round 137):** `--fresh` extracts skip-set names as backticked
`name_ts` tokens — entries logged WITHOUT the `_ts` suffix (most of the round-133..136
